    *   The methods and techniques proposed could be used in other SR and image restoration tasks.
    *   The work highlights the importance of multilingual considerations in SR research.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7**. The paper presents a solid contribution to scene text image super-resolution. The method is novel in its combination of multilingual priors, diffusion models, and OCR guidance. The experiments clearly demonstrate the superiority of the proposed approach. However, the novelty is somewhat incremental. The paper could be improved by a more detailed analysis of the computational complexity, as well as a deeper discussion of the method's limitations and failure cases. The use of UTF-8 is a valuable way to achieve the multi-lingual task.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### ContextQFormer: A New Context Modeling Method for Multi-Turn Multi-Modal Conversations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23121v1)
- **Authors**: Yiming Lei, Zhizheng Yang, Zeming Liu, Haitao Leng, Shaoguo Liu, Tingting Gao, Qingjie Liu, Yunhong Wang
- **Abstract**: Multi-modal large language models have demonstrated remarkable zero-shot abilities and powerful image-understanding capabilities. However, the existing open-source multi-modal models suffer from the weak capability of multi-turn interaction, especially for long contexts. To address the issue, we first introduce a context modeling module, termed ContextQFormer, which utilizes a memory block to enhance the presentation of contextual information. Furthermore, to facilitate further research, we carefully build a new multi-turn multi-modal dialogue dataset (TMDialog) for pre-training, instruction-tuning, and evaluation, which will be open-sourced lately. Compared with other multi-modal dialogue datasets, TMDialog contains longer conversations, which supports the research of multi-turn multi-modal dialogue. In addition, ContextQFormer is compared with three baselines on TMDialog and experimental results illustrate that ContextQFormer achieves an improvement of 2%-4% in available rate over baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ContextQFormer: A New Context Modeling Method for Multi-Turn Multi-Modal Conversations":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ContextQFormer, a novel context modeling method aimed at improving the multi-turn interaction capabilities of multi-modal large language models (MLLMs), especially in long contexts. The key contributions are:

1.  **ContextQFormer Module:** This module uses a memory block to better represent and incorporate contextual information in MLLMs. It fuses the context embedding with the memory block to obtain a richer context representation.
2.  **TMDialog Dataset:** A new multi-turn multi-modal dialogue dataset, TMDialog, is created for pre-training, instruction-tuning, and evaluation of MLLMs. This dataset is designed to contain longer conversations, addressing the scarcity of such datasets.
3.  **Experimental Results:** The paper compares ContextQFormer with baselines (mPLUG-owl, visualGLM, and a LoRA-only version) on TMDialog, demonstrating improved performance in terms of available rate (a metric combining rationality and freedom from hallucinations).

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a Relevant Problem:** The paper tackles a recognized limitation of existing MLLMs: their difficulty in handling long-context, multi-turn conversations.  This is a significant challenge for real-world applications.
*   **Novel Method:** The ContextQFormer module provides a reasonable approach to incorporate context using a memory block and attention mechanism, which improves the model's performance.
*   **Valuable Dataset:** The creation and release of the TMDialog dataset fills a gap in the availability of multi-turn multi-modal datasets. The dataset's longer conversation lengths are a valuable addition.
*   **Clear Experiments:** The experiments are well-defined, and the baselines are relevant. The use of GPT-4 for evaluation is understandable, though indirect due to the inability to directly pass the image. The metrics are appropriate.
*   **Incremental Improvement:** The paper acknowledges the incremental nature of the improvement, providing a clear understanding of the achieved gains.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** The ContextQFormer module, while sensible, builds upon existing techniques like Q-Former (from BLIP-2) and attention mechanisms. The novelty lies primarily in the specific combination and application to this particular problem, rather than a revolutionary new architecture.
*   **Indirect Evaluation:** The reliance on GPT-4 to evaluate based on descriptions of images is a workaround, not ideal. It introduces a layer of abstraction and potential bias.  Ideally, direct evaluation with visual input would be preferable.
*   **Dataset Quality Concerns:**  While the TMDialog dataset is a valuable contribution, the fact that a significant portion is generated using GPT-4 raises concerns about potential biases, hallucination propagation, and lack of genuine human interaction diversity as noted in the limitation section.
*   **Limited Scale of Experiment:** The models were run with only 7 billion parameters due to resource restrictions. Given the trend toward extremely large models, it's difficult to assess how well ContextQFormer would scale.  The experiments are also restricted to V100 GPUs, which might not fully capture the potential performance improvements possible with newer hardware.
*   **Limited Scope:** Focus is restricted to vision-language dialogue. Other modalities are not explored.
*   **Limited Discussion of Limitations:** The paper mentions some limitations in Section 6 (Limation) which is positive, but the discussion could be more in-depth. For example, there is not much discussion of potential issues from the queueing mechanism in the context model, like time complexity, though it is stated in the last paragraph.
*   **Potential Bias:** The paper mentions an additional measure of adding naturally annotated language, but it is not explicitly explained how or why this addition has the effect it does.

**Significance:**

The paper has the potential to influence the development of more capable MLLMs for interactive applications. The TMDialog dataset will likely be a useful resource for other researchers in the field. However, the incremental nature of the method and the dataset quality concerns limit the overall impact. The work provides a solid step in the right direction but does not represent a breakthrough.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:**

A score of 6 is justified because the paper makes a valuable, albeit incremental, contribution to the field. The ContextQFormer module offers a practical approach to improve context handling in MLLMs, and the TMDialog dataset addresses a need for longer multi-modal dialogue data. However, the novelty is limited, the evaluation relies on a workaround, and there are potential concerns about dataset quality and the impact from the lack of scale in the experiments. The paper pushes the field forward, but not in a dramatically transformative way.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### PBEBench: A Multi-Step Programming by Examples Reasoning Benchmark inspired by Historical Linguistics
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23126v1)
- **Authors**: Atharva Naik, Darsh Agrawal, Manav Kapadnis, Yuwei An, Yash Mathur, Carolyn Rose, David Mortensen
- **Abstract**: Recently, long chain of thought (LCoT), Large Language Models (LLMs), have taken the machine learning world by storm with their breathtaking reasoning capabilities. However, are the abstract reasoning abilities of these models general enough for problems of practical importance? Unlike past work, which has focused mainly on math, coding, and data wrangling, we focus on a historical linguistics-inspired inductive reasoning problem, formulated as Programming by Examples. We develop a fully automated pipeline for dynamically generating a benchmark for this task with controllable difficulty in order to tackle scalability and contamination issues to which many reasoning benchmarks are subject. Using our pipeline, we generate a test set with nearly 1k instances that is challenging for all state-of-the-art reasoning LLMs, with the best model (Claude-3.7-Sonnet) achieving a mere 54% pass rate, demonstrating that LCoT LLMs still struggle with a class or reasoning that is ubiquitous in historical linguistics as well as many other domains.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PBEBench, a novel benchmark designed to evaluate the reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) in a task inspired by historical linguistics. This task, "forward reconstruction," involves inferring a sequence of string rewrite rules (programs) that transform input words from an ancestor language into corresponding words in modern languages, given examples of input-output pairs. The benchmark generation process is fully automated, allowing for controllable difficulty and scalability, thus addressing common issues like data leakage in existing benchmarks. The authors demonstrate that even state-of-the-art LLMs struggle with this task, achieving a modest pass rate, suggesting that current LLMs may not be robust in complex reasoning involving inductive program synthesis and planning. Furthermore, the authors analyze the correlation between various complexity features of the benchmark instances and the empirical difficulty observed by the models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main contribution lies in the novel task and the automated benchmark generation pipeline.  While programming by example is a well-established area, the application to a problem inspired by historical linguistics and its formulation as a multi-step string rewrite rule induction problem represents a fresh perspective.  The development of provably correct algorithms for detecting relationships (feeding/bleeding) between string rewrite rules is also a solid contribution.
*   **Significance:** The benchmark is significant because it moves beyond typical LLM evaluation domains like mathematics, coding, or data wrangling. It targets a more abstract and potentially transferable reasoning skill - inductive program synthesis - that's relevant to various fields. The fact that LLMs perform poorly suggests a gap in their ability to handle these types of problems and highlights an area for future research. The dataset's scalability and controllability are crucial for further investigating LLM weaknesses and mitigating contamination.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   Well-defined problem formulation that is readily understood.
    *   Fully automated benchmark generation and ability to control complexity.
    *   Provably correct algorithms for detecting feeding/bleeding relationships, a key component of task difficulty.
    *   Empirical results that clearly demonstrate the challenge for current LLMs.
    *   Analysis of the relationship between instance complexity and difficulty.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The benchmark seems to generate a large number of degenerate programs (programs with no impact), which skews the complexity of the dataset toward simpler instances. This potentially limits the exploration of model capabilities on more complex scenarios.  The dataset statistics show that more than 80% of the programs have one to zero dependencies.
    *   The analysis of the complexity features, while thorough, reveals a relatively weak correlation between complexity features and model performance. There's a possibility of a complex relationship between difficulty features and empirical model outputs.
    *   The evaluation primarily relies on pass@1 and edit distance.  More sophisticated evaluation metrics capturing the quality of the induced program sequence (e.g., measures of program conciseness, elegance) could further strengthen the analysis.

*   **Impact:** The PBEBench has the potential to become a valuable resource for the LLM research community. It provides a challenging and controllable benchmark for evaluating and improving the reasoning abilities of LLMs. It can also encourage research into better alignment between LLMs and human needs in practical domains, such as knowledge acquisition and scientific discovery.

*   **Justification of Score:** While the paper has strengths in task novelty and automated benchmark creation, the dataset's limited complexity due to degenerate programs and the somewhat weak correlations found in the complexity analysis slightly temper the overall impact. It is also important to address that the study of relationships and ordering between string rewrite rules, although significant for historical linguistics, is a rather narrow application of inductive program synthesis. A more general PBE environment might be more impactful.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### VERINA: Benchmarking Verifiable Code Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23135v1)
- **Authors**: Zhe Ye, Zhengxu Yan, Jingxuan He, Timothe Kasriel, Kaiyu Yang, Dawn Song
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly integrated in software development, but ensuring correctness in LLM-generated code remains challenging and often requires costly manual review. Verifiable code generation -- jointly generating code, specifications, and proofs of code-specification alignment -- offers a promising path to address this limitation and further unleash LLMs' benefits in coding. Yet, there exists a significant gap in evaluation: current benchmarks often lack support for end-to-end verifiable code generation. In this paper, we introduce Verina (Verifiable Code Generation Arena), a high-quality benchmark enabling a comprehensive and modular evaluation of code, specification, and proof generation as well as their compositions. Verina consists of 189 manually curated coding tasks in Lean, with detailed problem descriptions, reference implementations, formal specifications, and extensive test suites. Our extensive evaluation of state-of-the-art LLMs reveals significant challenges in verifiable code generation, especially in proof generation, underscoring the need for improving LLM-based theorem provers in verification domains. The best model, OpenAI o4-mini, generates only 61.4% correct code, 51.0% sound and complete specifications, and 3.6% successful proofs, with one trial per task. We hope Verina will catalyze progress in verifiable code generation by providing a rigorous and comprehensive benchmark. We release our dataset on https://huggingface.co/datasets/sunblaze-ucb/verina and our evaluation code on https://github.com/sunblaze-ucb/verina.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the VERINA paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VERINA (Verifiable Code Generation Arena), a new benchmark for evaluating large language models (LLMs) in the context of verifiable code generation. VERINA is designed to comprehensively assess the generation of code, formal specifications, and proofs of their alignment. It consists of 189 manually curated coding tasks in Lean, each including problem descriptions, reference implementations, specifications, and test suites. The authors evaluate several state-of-the-art LLMs on VERINA, revealing significant challenges in verifiable code generation, especially in proof generation. The benchmark aims to catalyze progress in the field by providing a rigorous evaluation platform.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty of the paper lies in the creation of a comprehensive benchmark specifically designed for *verifiable* code generation. While code generation benchmarks exist (e.g., HumanEval, MBPP), they don't focus on generating formal specifications and formal proofs of correctness alongside the code. VERINA addresses this gap, providing a more complete picture of a system's ability to produce trustworthy software. Clover and Dafny Synthesis attempt similar goals in the context of SMT-based verification with fewer data points. The key novelty lies in the choice of the interactive theorem prover Lean, combined with a larger, high-quality dataset with three key elements: code, specification and proof.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from the increasing integration of LLMs in software development and the crucial need for ensuring the correctness of LLM-generated code. By introducing a benchmark that directly targets verifiable code generation, the authors are pushing the field towards more reliable and trustworthy AI-assisted coding tools. The experimental results highlight the limitations of current LLMs in proof generation, which is a valuable insight that can guide future research efforts. Furthermore, this will enable more accurate testing of these models in terms of soundness and completeness, which could not be done previously.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Benchmark:** VERINA offers a complete set of artifacts (code, specs, proofs, tests) for evaluating verifiable code generation.
    *   **High-Quality Dataset:** The manual curation process ensures the quality and correctness of the benchmark samples.
    *   **Focus on Lean:** The use of Lean is well-justified due to its interactive theorem proving capabilities, offering a different perspective compared to SMT-based verification.
    *   **Modular Evaluation:** VERINA allows for flexible evaluation of code, specification, and proof generation, both individually and in combination.
    *   **Practical Evaluation Metrics:** The paper proposes a testing-based approach to evaluate specification quality.
    *   **Open Source Availability:** The benchmark and evaluation code are publicly available, promoting reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Size:** While the benchmark is high-quality, 189 examples might be a limiting factor, especially for fine-tuning LLMs.
    *   **Domain Specificity:** The tasks are primarily standalone coding problems, which might not fully reflect the complexity of real-world software development scenarios. While this improves precision in testing, broader context and larger examples are needed for real world application.
    *   **Metric limitations:** The testing based approach to evaluating specification quality (soundness and completeness) might miss some subtle errors in specifications. The paper acknowledges this, but it is still a limitation. A small but important subset of the test cases are undecidable by the "decide" tactic and thus relies on property based testing. While useful, property based testing has its own limitations.
    *   **Data Contamination:** The authors acknowledge the risk of data contamination due to the use of widely used problem descriptions. While effort was put into mitigating this, there is a non-zero probability that future tests results will show artificially inflated performance due to this factor.

*   **Potential Influence:** VERINA has the potential to significantly influence research on LLM-assisted software development, particularly in areas such as formal methods, automated verification, and trustworthy AI. It provides a valuable resource for researchers to compare and improve their models' capabilities in generating verifiable code. It helps standardize testing metrics, and enables new testing paradigms previously unavailable.
**Score: 8**

**Justification:**

VERINA represents a significant step forward in benchmarking verifiable code generation. The creation of a comprehensive, high-quality benchmark with a focus on formal specifications and proofs is a valuable contribution to the field. The experimental results highlight the limitations of current LLMs and provide valuable insights for future research. While the benchmark has some limitations in terms of size and domain specificity, its strengths outweigh its weaknesses, making it a highly significant resource for the community. The score of 8 reflects the novelty, significance, and potential influence of the paper, while acknowledging its limitations and the areas where future work could improve upon its contributions.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Enhancing Large Language Models'Machine Translation via Dynamic Focus Anchoring
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23140v1)
- **Authors**: Qiuyu Ding, Zhiqiang Cao, Hailong Cao, Tiejun Zhao
- **Abstract**: Large language models have demonstrated exceptional performance across multiple crosslingual NLP tasks, including machine translation (MT). However, persistent challenges remain in addressing context-sensitive units (CSUs), such as polysemous words. These CSUs not only affect the local translation accuracy of LLMs, but also affect LLMs' understanding capability for sentences and tasks, and even lead to translation failure. To address this problem, we propose a simple but effective method to enhance LLMs' MT capabilities by acquiring CSUs and applying semantic focus. Specifically, we dynamically analyze and identify translation challenges, then incorporate them into LLMs in a structured manner to mitigate mistranslations or misunderstandings of CSUs caused by information flattening. Efficiently activate LLMs to identify and apply relevant knowledge from its vast data pool in this way, ensuring more accurate translations for translating difficult terms. On a benchmark dataset of MT, our proposed method achieved competitive performance compared to multiple existing open-sourced MT baseline models. It demonstrates effectiveness and robustness across multiple language pairs, including both similar language pairs and distant language pairs. Notably, the proposed method requires no additional model training and enhances LLMs' performance across multiple NLP tasks with minimal resource consumption.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Dynamic Focus Anchoring (DFA), a resource-efficient method to enhance large language models (LLMs) for machine translation (MT).  DFA addresses the "semantic ambiguity" problem caused by context-sensitive units (CSUs) like polysemous words, domain-specific terms, and culturally-specific expressions. The method involves two stages: (1) identifying and classifying CSUs using both external linguistic resources and internal knowledge activation within the LLM, and (2) injecting semantic focus into a dynamically structured prompt used for translation, guiding the LLM to focus on these specific CSU types. The paper demonstrates that DFA improves translation accuracy, particularly for distant language pairs, without requiring model retraining or extensive parallel data. It focuses primarily on modifying the prompting strategy rather than fine-tuning the underlying LLM.  The paper includes experiments on WMT benchmark datasets for English-German and English-Chinese translation, demonstrating improvements over several existing LLM-based MT baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addressing a Relevant Problem:** The paper tackles a persistent and important issue in LLM-based MT: the challenges of handling context-sensitive units (CSUs).  The paper convincingly argues that LLMs often struggle with ambiguity and context-dependent translation, and provides a clear, illustrative example (the "bank" sentence).
    *   **Resource Efficiency:**  A major strength is the method's efficiency.  It enhances translation without requiring costly model training or parallel data, making it accessible and practical. This is a significant benefit, especially considering the computational demands of LLMs.
    *   **Novelty in Prompting Strategy:** The approach of dynamically identifying and highlighting CSUs in the prompt is a novel strategy. It moves beyond static prompt engineering and demonstrates the value of adaptive prompting based on the specific sentence to be translated.
    *   **Experimental Validation:**  The experimental results show consistent improvements over strong baseline systems (especially Bayling2), across both similar and distant language pairs. The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of different components of the DFA method. The inclusion of COMET and chrF2 alongside BLEU strengthens the evaluation.
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The paper provides a clear and detailed explanation of the DFA method, including the CSU identification process and the semantic focus injection mechanism.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited CSU Types:** While the paper addresses polysemous, domain-specific, and cultural CSUs, it acknowledges that these are not exhaustive. Expanding the method to handle other types of CSUs could further improve performance.
    *   **Reliance on External Resources:** The CSU identification relies partially on external linguistic resources (e.g., MUSE dataset). The performance is thus directly tied to the quality and coverage of these resources. A completely internal CSU identification method would be preferable. While the internal knowledge activation component exists, external data helps for initial grounding/focusing. This is still a strength of the method since it explicitly states its intention of the design being a bidirectional coupled system.
    *   **Modest Improvement:** While statistically significant, the improvements are not *dramatic*. A more substantial performance leap would significantly increase the impact.
    *   **Limited to Initial Sentence:** The method only uses the first sentence for the evaluation after translation. The potential effects of the focus injections on the entire translated text/paragraph are not analyzed.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** While the method strives for resource efficiency, the analysis of *k* (CSU number) suggests the method can be parameter-sensitive. Demonstrating its robustness with different *k* values and datasets would increase confidence. The "optimality" statement should be investigated further.

*   **Significance:**

    The significance lies in providing a practical and effective way to improve LLM-based MT without massive retraining. The method offers a valuable contribution to the field by demonstrating the power of dynamically adapting prompts to address specific translation challenges. The framework also gives useful insights into how LLMs can be controlled or "forced" into utilizing their knowledge effectively.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper demonstrates a good level of novelty and significance. The DFA method tackles a relevant problem in LLM-based MT with a resource-efficient approach that delivers measurable performance gains. The dynamic prompting strategy shows promise for improving the accuracy of LLMs by drawing their attention toward contextually tricky words. However, the improvements are still somewhat incremental. Furthermore, there is room for expanding the method's coverage of CSU types, fully removing its reliance on external resources, and more rigorous robustness/sensitivity analysis with respect to the parameters used. Despite the modest improvement and some limitations, DFA offers a practical and insightful contribution to the LLM-based MT research, making it a valuable piece of work.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Implicit Inversion turns CLIP into a Decoder
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23161v1)
- **Authors**: Antonio D'Orazio, Maria Rosaria Briglia, Donato Crisostomi, Dario Loi, Emanuele Rodolà, Iacopo Masi
- **Abstract**: CLIP is a discriminative model trained to align images and text in a shared embedding space. Due to its multimodal structure, it serves as the backbone of many generative pipelines, where a decoder is trained to map from the shared space back to images. In this work, we show that image synthesis is nevertheless possible using CLIP alone -- without any decoder, training, or fine-tuning. Our approach optimizes a frequency-aware implicit neural representation that encourages coarse-to-fine generation by stratifying frequencies across network layers. To stabilize this inverse mapping, we introduce adversarially robust initialization, a lightweight Orthogonal Procrustes projection to align local text and image embeddings, and a blending loss that anchors outputs to natural image statistics. Without altering CLIP's weights, this framework unlocks capabilities such as text-to-image generation, style transfer, and image reconstruction. These findings suggest that discriminative models may hold untapped generative potential, hidden in plain sight.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Implicit Inversion turns CLIP into a Decoder":

**Summary:**

The paper presents CLIP⁻¹, a novel framework that leverages CLIP (a pre-trained vision-language model) for text-to-image generation *without* requiring any additional training, fine-tuning, or a dedicated generative decoder. Instead, CLIP⁻¹ inverts CLIP's image encoder by optimizing a frequency-aware Implicit Neural Representation (INR).  The method uses adversarial robust initialization, and an Orthogonal Procrustes projection to bridge the modality gap between text and image embeddings, and blending loss to guide the image synthesis. The approach unlocks capabilities like text-to-image generation, style transfer, and image reconstruction, demonstrating that discriminative models can hold untapped generative potential.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core idea of directly inverting CLIP to generate images is not entirely novel. Prior works have attempted this (mentioned in the paper), but the authors significantly improve the image quality and stability of the inversion process. The key novel contributions lie in:

    *   *Frequency-Aware INR Optimization:*  Stratifying the optimization process across network layers to control frequency bands and generate images in a coarse-to-fine manner is a key contribution. It helps stabilize the optimization and avoid artifacts often seen in direct pixel-space optimization.
    *   *Adversarial Robust Initialization:*  Using INRs pre-trained with AWP provides a robust anchor for the inversion process, improving stability and image quality. The use of AWP for initialization is a plus.
    *   *Modality Gap Reduction with Procrustes:*  The Orthogonal Procrustes projection to align text and image embeddings addresses a crucial limitation of CLIP, stabilizing the inversion process by projecting the text embedding into the image manifold, leading to more semantically aligned images.
    *   *Full Training-Free Inversion:* Previous attempts often involved fine-tuning CLIP or using training to augment image quality. This paper shows text-to-image with zero training or fine-tuning

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper's findings are significant because they challenge the conventional wisdom of needing dedicated generative decoders for text-to-image synthesis. Showing that a frozen discriminative model like CLIP can be repurposed for image generation has broader implications for model interpretability and reuse. It suggests that large pre-trained models might have untapped generative potential.
    *   The approach is particularly appealing due to its training-free and decoder-free nature, making it accessible and computationally efficient. This simplifies the text-to-image pipeline and reduces the reliance on complex, resource-intensive generative models.
    *   The applications demonstrated (style transfer, image reconstruction, controlled edits) further highlight the versatility of the framework.
    *   The paper contributes to a growing body of research on inverting pre-trained models. However, the images generated are not at the level of state-of-the-art models like DALL-E3 or Imagen. The primary impact is conceptual and methodological, showing a different way to approach text-to-image generation.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem statement and motivation.
    *   Well-explained methodology with detailed descriptions of the key components.
    *   Comprehensive experimental validation, including quantitative comparisons with existing methods and ablation studies.
    *   Qualitative results demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach and the impact of each component.
    *   The paper is well-written and easy to follow.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Image quality, while significantly improved compared to previous inversion methods, still lags behind state-of-the-art generative models.  This limits the practical applicability of the method in its current form.
    *   The reliance on INRs, while contributing to the stability of the inversion, can introduce artifacts or distortions in fine-grained details, as noted in the paper.
    *   The approach relies on MS-COCO which has a narrow range of concepts and visual styles and this does not give a full view of how well it can work.
    *   Evaluation only shows CLIP-based metrics and is not using the newer more rigorous evaluations that use human raters.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper can significantly impact the field by inspiring new research directions in generative modeling and model inversion. The ideas presented could be integrated into existing generative pipelines or used as a basis for developing more efficient and interpretable text-to-image models. Further research could focus on improving the image quality of the inversions, exploring different INR architectures, or extending the approach to other modalities.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a *notably* novel method for text-to-image generation by inverting CLIP, achieving good visual results without any training or fine-tuning. The integration of frequency-aware INR optimization, robust initialization, and modality gap reduction is well-motivated and effective. The rigorous experimental validation and ablation studies support the claims made. While image quality isn't fully state of the art, the conceptual contribution and the simplicity of the framework are valuable. The paper opens up new opportunities for exploring generative capabilities of pre-trained models. The potential for future research directions earns a solid score, but the current image quality and the need for additional validation with more diverse datasets and human raters prevent a higher score. The strengths are significant, but the limitations prevent the paper from being considered a truly exceptional contribution that would significantly reshape the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Infinite-Instruct: Synthesizing Scaling Code instruction Data with Bidirectional Synthesis and Static Verification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23177v1)
- **Authors**: Wenjing Xing, Wenke Lu, Yeheng Duan, Bing Zhao, Zhenghui kang, Yaolong Wang, Kai Gao, Lei Qiao
- **Abstract**: Traditional code instruction data synthesis methods suffer from limited diversity and poor logic. We introduce Infinite-Instruct, an automated framework for synthesizing high-quality question-answer pairs, designed to enhance the code generation capabilities of large language models (LLMs). The framework focuses on improving the internal logic of synthesized problems and the quality of synthesized code. First, "Reverse Construction" transforms code snippets into diverse programming problems. Then, through "Backfeeding Construction," keywords in programming problems are structured into a knowledge graph to reconstruct them into programming problems with stronger internal logic. Finally, a cross-lingual static code analysis pipeline filters invalid samples to ensure data quality. Experiments show that on mainstream code generation benchmarks, our fine-tuned models achieve an average performance improvement of 21.70% on 7B-parameter models and 36.95% on 32B-parameter models. Using less than one-tenth of the instruction fine-tuning data, we achieved performance comparable to the Qwen-2.5-Coder-Instruct. Infinite-Instruct provides a scalable solution for LLM training in programming. We open-source the datasets used in the experiments, including both unfiltered versions and filtered versions via static analysis. The data are available at https://github.com/xingwenjing417/Infinite-Instruct-dataset
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Infinite-Instruct: Synthesizing Scaling Code instruction Data with Bidirectional Synthesis and Static Verification":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Infinite-Instruct," a novel framework for automatically synthesizing high-quality code instruction data to improve the code generation capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs). It addresses limitations of existing methods, such as limited diversity and poor logic, by employing a bidirectional approach.  The framework consists of two main components:

1.  **Reverse Construction:**  Transforms existing code snippets into diverse programming problems.
2.  **Backfeeding Construction:**  Structures keywords extracted from these problems into a knowledge graph to create new programming tasks with stronger internal logic.

A static code analysis pipeline filters the generated data to ensure quality. Experiments show significant performance improvements on standard code generation benchmarks when fine-tuning models with data generated by Infinite-Instruct. Specifically, performance gains are reported for 7B and 32B parameter models, even when using a relatively small fraction of the data compared to other approaches. The datasets and code are made publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The novelty of the paper lies in the integration of several ideas:

*   **Bidirectional Synthesis:**  The combination of "reverse construction" (code-to-problem) and "backfeeding construction" (keywords-to-problem) presents a novel approach to data generation, moving beyond simple evolution or LLM-driven prompt generation.
*   **Knowledge Graph Integration:** The use of a knowledge graph to structure and refine keywords extracted from synthesized problems adds a layer of logical reasoning and coherence to the generated instructions.  This is a valuable addition compared to simply using randomized keywords.
*   **Comprehensive Static Analysis:**  The seven-dimensional quality assessment protocol, leveraging cross-lingual static code analysis, is a significant step towards ensuring the validity and usability of the generated instruction data. This helps in eliminating errors and increases the trustworthiness of the data used for fine-tuning.

**Significance:** The paper's significance stems from addressing a critical bottleneck in scaling LLM training for code generation: the availability of high-quality instruction data. The automated nature of Infinite-Instruct, coupled with the improved quality of the generated data (validated by performance gains), offers a practical and scalable solution. The results demonstrating comparable performance to Qwen-2.5-Coder-Instruct with significantly less data is noteworthy, showcasing the efficiency of the proposed framework.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-Defined Framework:** The methodology is clearly explained, with distinct components and a logical flow.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experiments are conducted on multiple benchmark datasets and with different model sizes, providing a robust evaluation of the framework's effectiveness.
*   **Open Source:**  The release of the datasets is a valuable contribution to the community, facilitating further research and development in this area.
*   **Performance Improvement:** The significant performance gains reported on established code generation benchmarks provide solid evidence supporting the effectiveness of the proposed method.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Difficulty Adjustment Mechanism:** The paper acknowledges that the difficulty adjustment mechanism is simplistic and could be improved. The current strategy, based solely on the number of keywords, may not accurately capture the complexity of the generated problems.
*   **Limitations of Static Analysis:** Static analysis, while useful, cannot detect all types of code issues. The framework may miss logical errors or algorithmic inefficiencies.  This reliance on static analysis, while improving quality, might also limit the variety of problems generated.
*   **Imbalanced Multi-language Support:**  The varying levels of support for different programming languages could introduce biases in the generated data and limit the generalizability of the results.
*   **Lack of Ablation Studies:** While the paper shows that the combined approach of Reverse and Backfeed construction performs well, more in-depth ablation studies comparing Reverse vs Backfeed vs the combination would further justify the architectural choices and highlight the individual contributions.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of code generation by providing a scalable and effective method for creating high-quality instruction data.  The framework's ability to improve the performance of LLMs with relatively less data makes it particularly valuable. Further research could focus on improving the difficulty adjustment mechanism, expanding multi-language support, and integrating dynamic analysis to address the limitations of static analysis.  The insights from this work could also be applied to other domains beyond code generation.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of code generation by introducing a practical and effective framework for automated instruction data synthesis. The strong experimental results and the release of the datasets are major strengths. However, the limitations regarding the difficulty adjustment, reliance on static analysis, and imbalanced multi-language support prevent it from achieving a higher score. While it significantly advances the state-of-the-art, more sophisticated difficulty adjustment and a broader range of code issues detection methods would be needed for a higher score. This score reflects a solid advancement with clear potential for further refinement and significant impact on the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### DIP-R1: Deep Inspection and Perception with RL Looking Through and Understanding Complex Scenes
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23179v1)
- **Authors**: Sungjune Park, Hyunjun Kim, Junho Kim, Seongho Kim, Yong Man Ro
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have demonstrated significant visual understanding capabilities, yet their fine-grained visual perception in complex real-world scenarios, such as densely crowded public areas, remains limited. Inspired by the recent success of reinforcement learning (RL) in both LLMs and MLLMs, in this paper, we explore how RL can enhance visual perception ability of MLLMs. Then we develop a novel RL-based framework, Deep Inspection and Perception with RL (DIP-R1) designed to enhance the visual perception capabilities of MLLMs, by comprehending complex scenes and looking through visual instances closely. DIP-R1 guides MLLMs through detailed inspection of visual scene via three simply designed rule-based reward modelings. First, we adopt a standard reasoning reward encouraging the model to include three step-by-step processes: 1) reasoning for understanding visual scenes, 2) observing for looking through interested but ambiguous regions, and 3) decision-making for predicting answer. Second, a variance-guided looking reward is designed to examine uncertain regions for the second observing process. It explicitly enables the model to inspect ambiguous areas, improving its ability to mitigate perceptual uncertainties. Third, we model a weighted precision-recall accuracy reward enhancing accurate decision-making. We explore its effectiveness across diverse fine-grained object detection data consisting of challenging real-world environments, such as densely crowded scenes. Built upon existing MLLMs, DIP-R1 achieves consistent and significant improvement across various in-domain and out-of-domain scenarios. It also outperforms various existing baseline models and supervised fine-tuning methods. Our findings highlight the substantial potential of integrating RL into MLLMs for enhancing capabilities in complex real-world perception tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the DIP-R1 paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Deep Inspection and Perception with RL (DIP-R1), a reinforcement learning-based framework designed to enhance the visual perception capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) in complex scenes. DIP-R1 guides MLLMs to closely inspect visual instances by employing three rule-based reward modelings: a reasoning reward, a variance-guided looking reward (focusing on uncertain regions), and a weighted precision-recall accuracy reward. The framework achieves improved performance on object detection in challenging real-world environments, outperforming existing baselines and supervised fine-tuning methods on datasets like CrowdHuman, CityPersons, WiderPedestrian, and UAVDT.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Targeting a significant limitation:** The paper directly addresses a relevant weakness of current MLLMs: the difficulty in fine-grained visual perception within complex and densely crowded scenes. This is a crucial challenge for real-world applications.
    *   **Effective RL Approach:** The use of Reinforcement Learning to guide visual inspection is a promising approach, and the proposed reward functions appear thoughtfully designed. The variance-guided looking reward, in particular, is a novel and intuitive way to focus attention on ambiguous regions.
    *   **Consistent and Significant Improvement:** Experimental results demonstrate consistent performance gains across various datasets and scenarios, suggesting the robustness of the DIP-R1 framework.
    *   **Well-defined ablation study:** The ablation provides insight on the relative importance of each of components of the framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Rule-based Rewards:** The design of reward functions is still handcrafted and rule-based, which might limit its generalizability across different tasks. There might be a better learning-based reward functions.
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of RL and vision-language models is growing, the core elements used aren't groundbreaking on their own. The primary novelty lies in the specific combination and tailoring of these elements.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The paper could benefit from a more explicit discussion of the computational overhead associated with RL fine-tuning, especially considering the already high computational demands of MLLMs.
    *   **Limited Scope:** Focus is strictly object detection and specifically on improvement on object detection. The discussion and results could be broader.

*   **Novelty and Significance:** The novelty lies in the specific combination of RL and a tailored set of reward functions to address the challenging problem of fine-grained visual perception in complex scenes. The results demonstrate a measurable improvement over existing methods, suggesting potential for advancing MLLM capabilities in this area. The application of RL is well reasoned and implemented and achieves noticeable gains.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper provides a valuable exploration of RL for enhancing MLLMs and can stimulate further research in this direction, particularly in exploring more sophisticated reward functions and broader applications.

**Justification:**
The paper presents a well-executed approach to improve object detection capabilities of MLLMs. The method improves previous approaches in that aspect. The limitation to vision and the incremental improvement prevents the assignment of a score higher than 6.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Unsupervised Word-level Quality Estimation for Machine Translation Through the Lens of Annotators (Dis)agreement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23183v1)
- **Authors**: Gabriele Sarti, Vilém Zouhar, Malvina Nissim, Arianna Bisazza
- **Abstract**: Word-level quality estimation (WQE) aims to automatically identify fine-grained error spans in machine-translated outputs and has found many uses, including assisting translators during post-editing. Modern WQE techniques are often expensive, involving prompting of large language models or ad-hoc training on large amounts of human-labeled data. In this work, we investigate efficient alternatives exploiting recent advances in language model interpretability and uncertainty quantification to identify translation errors from the inner workings of translation models. In our evaluation spanning 14 metrics across 12 translation directions, we quantify the impact of human label variation on metric performance by using multiple sets of human labels. Our results highlight the untapped potential of unsupervised metrics, the shortcomings of supervised methods when faced with label uncertainty, and the brittleness of single-annotator evaluation practices.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will summarize the paper and provide a critical evaluation of its novelty and significance, assigning a score with a rigorous justification.

**Summary**

The paper "Unsupervised Word-level Quality Estimation for Machine Translation Through the Lens of Annotators (Dis)agreement" explores unsupervised methods for word-level quality estimation (WQE) in machine translation (MT). Instead of relying on costly large language models (LLMs) or supervised training data, the authors investigate metrics derived from the internal workings of translation models, such as predictive distributions, vocabulary projections, and context mixing.  A key aspect of the work is the evaluation of these metrics across multiple human annotations, enabling the quantification of the impact of annotator disagreement on metric performance.  The results highlight the potential of unsupervised metrics, shortcomings of supervised methods in the face of label uncertainty, and the fragility of single-annotator evaluation practices. They find that some unsupervised methods, particularly those based on predictive distributions with uncertainty quantification, show promise, and propose techniques to improve supervised WQE, such as confidence weighting of scores. Finally, the paper offers recommendations for future WQE evaluations, emphasizing the importance of multiple annotators.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its focus on unsupervised methods for WQE, combined with a comprehensive evaluation that explicitly considers annotator disagreement. While previous works have explored unsupervised metrics and uncertainty quantification in MT, their application and rigorous evaluation at the word-level, with explicit consideration of the impact of multiple annotations, represent a significant contribution. The calibration of XCOMET via confidence weighting also adds a new angle to applying supervised metrics. However, the metrics themselves (Surprisal, Logit Lens, Attention Entropy) are not entirely novel, as they have been used in other MT contexts.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its practical implications and insights into the challenges of WQE. By demonstrating the potential of unsupervised metrics, the authors offer a more efficient and accessible alternative to costly LLM-based or supervised approaches, particularly in low-resource settings. The finding that supervised methods can be outperformed by unsupervised ones, depending on calibration and data variance (human), is significant. The explicit evaluation with multiple annotations is a crucial methodological contribution, highlighting the limitations of single-annotator evaluations and advocating for more robust evaluation practices. The paper also provides practical recommendations for the WQE community to improve evaluation and usage of the metrics. This has a direct impact on how MT systems are evaluated and improved.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive evaluation across multiple languages, models, and datasets.
    *   Rigorous methodology that explicitly addresses annotator disagreement.
    *   Practical recommendations for future WQE evaluations.
    *   Demonstration of the potential of unsupervised methods.
    *   Interesting findings regarding the weaknesses of supervised methods in certain conditions.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The unsupervised metrics explored are not entirely novel.
    *   The reliance on existing datasets may limit the generalizability of the findings to other types of errors or domains.
    *   Some of the analysis, such as specific dataset choices, may come across as ad-hoc.
    *   While the impact of multiple annotators is clearly demonstrated, a deeper analysis of the *reasons* for disagreement could be valuable.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:
    *   Encouraging the exploration and development of more efficient unsupervised WQE methods.
    *   Promoting more robust evaluation practices that account for annotator disagreement.
    *   Highlighting the limitations of relying solely on supervised methods.
    *   Suggesting ways to improve the calibration and usability of existing metrics.

**Overall, the paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of machine translation by offering a comprehensive evaluation of unsupervised WQE methods and providing practical recommendations for improving evaluation practices. While the metrics themselves may not be entirely novel, the rigorous methodology and insights gained from explicitly considering annotator disagreement are significant.**

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper has clear strengths in terms of its methodology, practical implications, and potential to influence the field. While the individual metrics themselves aren't groundbreaking, the thoughtful and comprehensive analysis, and the critical focus on annotator agreement, make the paper a very valuable addition to the body of knowledge on WQE, warranting a higher than average score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Two Is Better Than One: Rotations Scale LoRAs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23184v1)
- **Authors**: Hongcan Guo, Guoshun Nan, Yuan Yang, Diyang Zhang, Haotian Li, Zhican Chen, Qinchuan Zhou, Yuhan Ran, Xinye Cao, Sicong Leng, Xiaofeng Tao, Xudong Jiang
- **Abstract**: Scaling Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA)-based Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) facilitates large language models (LLMs) to efficiently adapt to diverse tasks. However, traditional gating mechanisms that route inputs to the best experts may fundamentally hinder LLMs' scalability, leading to poor generalization and underfitting issues. We identify that the root cause lies in the restricted expressiveness of existing weighted-sum mechanisms, both within and outside the convex cone of LoRA representations. This motivates us to propose RadarGate, a novel geometrically inspired gating method that introduces rotational operations of LoRAs representations to boost the expressiveness and facilitate richer feature interactions among multiple LoRAs for scalable LLMs. Specifically, we first fuse each LoRA representation to other LoRAs using a learnable component and then feed the output to a rotation matrix. This matrix involves learnable parameters that define the relative angular relationship between LoRA representations. Such a simple yet effective mechanism provides an extra degree of freedom, facilitating the learning of cross-LoRA synergies and properly tracking the challenging poor generalization and underfitting issues as the number of LoRA grows. Extensive experiments on 6 public benchmarks across 21 tasks show the effectiveness of our RadarGate for scaling LoRAs. We also provide valuable insights, revealing that the rotations to each pair of representations are contrastive, encouraging closer alignment of semantically similar representations during geometrical transformation while pushing distance ones further apart. We will release our code to the community.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Two Is Better Than One: Rotations Scale LoRAs" addresses limitations in scaling Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA)-based Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) for large language models (LLMs). Existing gating mechanisms in LoRA-MoE architectures struggle with generalization and underfitting as the number of LoRA experts increases. The authors hypothesize that this stems from the restricted expressiveness of weighted-sum gating mechanisms, which confine LoRA outputs within a convex cone.  They propose RadarGate, a novel gating method that introduces rotational operations to LoRA representations, enhancing expressiveness and enabling richer feature interactions. RadarGate comprises a RotationGate (learning angles between LoRAs) and a StretchGate (adjusting magnitudes). The authors demonstrate empirically on several benchmark datasets the effectiveness of RadarGate in scaling LoRAs, improving generalization, and mitigating underfitting. They provide analysis suggesting RadarGate learns contrastive rotations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using rotations in the LoRA-MoE gating mechanism appears novel.  Existing approaches largely rely on weighted sums or rule-based combinations, so introducing a geometric transformation to boost expressiveness is a fresh take. The decoupling of angle learning (RotationGate) and magnitude scaling (StretchGate) further enhances the approach's flexibility.

*   **Significance:** The paper directly tackles a practical and important problem: scaling LoRA-MoE for LLMs. The issues of underfitting and poor generalization at scale are significant bottlenecks for deploying these architectures in real-world applications. Overcoming these limitations provides potential for better adaption and performance.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly articulates the problem and identifies a plausible root cause, which gives credibility to motivation.
    *   **Well-defined Method:** RadarGate is well-defined, and the components (RotationGate and StretchGate) are clearly explained.
    *   **Empirical Validation:**  Extensive experiments across a diverse set of benchmark datasets provide substantial evidence for the effectiveness of RadarGate.
    *   **Theoretical Justification:** The inclusion of theoretical lemmas, while not deeply rigorous, adds weight to the claims and provides some intuition behind the method's success.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies demonstrating the importance of both RotationGate and StretchGate contribute to understanding the method's mechanics.
    *   **Case study:** The inclusion of a case study showing how RadarGate is better to capture the latent architechure compared to MoE is a plus.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Theoretical Rigor:** While the lemmas offer a theoretical justification, they could be further developed. Some claims are still largely supported by empirical evidence.
    *   **Complexity and Overhead:** While the authors claim negligible overhead, a more thorough analysis of the computational cost compared to alternatives, particularly as the number of experts increases, is warranted. The low rank factorization helps, but overhead comparison against existing gating methods in large number of modules deserves attention.
    *   **Rotation Visualization Explanation:** Figure 5 and related explanations can be explained in a more details, providing visualization of the rotation that is implemented over the different LOAR modules and also what happens in the case when the rotation is not there.
    *   **Comparative Analysis:** While the paper compares against several baselines, a deeper discussion of the limitations of those baselines *in the context of the proposed hypothesis* would strengthen the argument.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence research in parameter-efficient fine-tuning by introducing a new direction for designing gating mechanisms.  The idea of incorporating geometric transformations might be applicable to other areas within LLM adaptation. If the method proves robust and widely applicable, it could become a standard technique for scaling LoRA-MoE architectures.

**Overall:**

The paper is a solid contribution to the field of parameter-efficient LLM adaptation. The approach is novel, well-motivated, and empirically supported. The theoretical justifications provide some support, although could be strengthened. While some points require further discussion and validation, the paper addresses a critical problem with a promising solution.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### HiGarment: Cross-modal Harmony Based Diffusion Model for Flat Sketch to Realistic Garment Image
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23186v1)
- **Authors**: Junyi Guo, Jingxuan Zhang, Fangyu Wu, Huanda Lu, Qiufeng Wang, Wenmian Yang, Eng Gee Lim, Dongming Lu
- **Abstract**: Diffusion-based garment synthesis tasks primarily focus on the design phase in the fashion domain, while the garment production process remains largely underexplored. To bridge this gap, we introduce a new task: Flat Sketch to Realistic Garment Image (FS2RG), which generates realistic garment images by integrating flat sketches and textual guidance. FS2RG presents two key challenges: 1) fabric characteristics are solely guided by textual prompts, providing insufficient visual supervision for diffusion-based models, which limits their ability to capture fine-grained fabric details; 2) flat sketches and textual guidance may provide conflicting information, requiring the model to selectively preserve or modify garment attributes while maintaining structural coherence. To tackle this task, we propose HiGarment, a novel framework that comprises two core components: i) a multi-modal semantic enhancement mechanism that enhances fabric representation across textual and visual modalities, and ii) a harmonized cross-attention mechanism that dynamically balances information from flat sketches and text prompts, allowing controllable synthesis by generating either sketch-aligned (image-biased) or text-guided (text-biased) outputs. Furthermore, we collect Multi-modal Detailed Garment, the largest open-source dataset for garment generation. Experimental results and user studies demonstrate the effectiveness of HiGarment in garment synthesis. The code and dataset will be released.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new task called Flat Sketch to Realistic Garment Image (FS2RG), aiming to generate realistic garment images from flat sketches and textual descriptions. The core idea is to bridge the gap between garment design and production by providing factories with realistic prototypes directly from designer sketches and desired fabric attributes. The paper highlights two key challenges: insufficient visual supervision for fine-grained fabric details and potential conflicts between flat sketches and textual guidance. To address these challenges, the authors propose HiGarment, a framework with two main components: a multi-modal semantic enhancement mechanism (MSE) that enhances fabric representation across modalities and a harmonized cross-attention mechanism (HCA) that dynamically balances information from sketches and text prompts. They also contribute a new dataset, Multi-Modal Detailed Garment (MMDGarment), to facilitate the research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The FS2RG task itself is a novel contribution. While garment synthesis is a well-explored area, the specific combination of flat sketches and text prompts to generate realistic production-ready images hasn't been extensively addressed. The HiGarment architecture, particularly the MSE and HCA components, represents a novel approach to handle multi-modal inputs and achieve modality harmony. The new dataset MMDGarment is also a welcome contribution to the field, addressing a gap in publicly available, richly annotated garment datasets.

* **Significance:** The potential impact of this work is significant. Bridging the gap between design and production could streamline the garment manufacturing process, reducing time and resource consumption. The FS2RG task provides a practical application for diffusion models in the fashion industry. If successful, this approach could revolutionize how garment prototypes are created and iterated. The new dataset enables further research in this area.

* **Strengths:**
    * Clear problem definition and motivation.
    * Novel task and architecture.
    * Contribution of a new, useful dataset.
    * Comprehensive experimental evaluation, including quantitative metrics and qualitative results. The ablation studies clearly demonstrate the importance of the proposed modules (MSE and HCA).
    * The paper is well-written and organized.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * The novelty is somewhat incremental. While the task is new, the core technologies (diffusion models, cross-attention) are well-established.
    * The improvements, while significant, are demonstrated within a relatively specific domain (garment synthesis). The generalizability of the approach to other multi-modal tasks isn't explored.
    * The paper acknowledges limitations regarding the preservation of tiny logos, highlighting a potential area for future improvement.
    * The description of fabric representation/retrieval could be expanded for clarity. It's important to carefully specify what attributes are encoded in the keys and how they are used in the retrieval mechanism.

* **Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of garment synthesis by introducing a new, practically motivated task and a novel architecture to address the challenges of multi-modal input. The new dataset is a welcome addition that will likely facilitate future research. While the core technologies are based on existing methods, the specific combination and adaptation to the FS2RG task are novel. The results show significant improvements over existing methods, indicating the effectiveness of the proposed approach.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### TrackVLA: Embodied Visual Tracking in the Wild
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23189v1)
- **Authors**: Shaoan Wang, Jiazhao Zhang, Minghan Li, Jiahang Liu, Anqi Li, Kui Wu, Fangwei Zhong, Junzhi Yu, Zhizheng Zhang, He Wang
- **Abstract**: Embodied visual tracking is a fundamental skill in Embodied AI, enabling an agent to follow a specific target in dynamic environments using only egocentric vision. This task is inherently challenging as it requires both accurate target recognition and effective trajectory planning under conditions of severe occlusion and high scene dynamics. Existing approaches typically address this challenge through a modular separation of recognition and planning. In this work, we propose TrackVLA, a Vision-Language-Action (VLA) model that learns the synergy between object recognition and trajectory planning. Leveraging a shared LLM backbone, we employ a language modeling head for recognition and an anchor-based diffusion model for trajectory planning. To train TrackVLA, we construct an Embodied Visual Tracking Benchmark (EVT-Bench) and collect diverse difficulty levels of recognition samples, resulting in a dataset of 1.7 million samples. Through extensive experiments in both synthetic and real-world environments, TrackVLA demonstrates SOTA performance and strong generalizability. It significantly outperforms existing methods on public benchmarks in a zero-shot manner while remaining robust to high dynamics and occlusion in real-world scenarios at 10 FPS inference speed. Our project page is: https://pku-epic.github.io/TrackVLA-web.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the TrackVLA paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TrackVLA, a Vision-Language-Action (VLA) model for embodied visual tracking in dynamic environments.  TrackVLA integrates object recognition and trajectory planning into a unified framework, leveraging a shared LLM backbone. A language modeling head handles recognition, while an anchor-based diffusion model handles trajectory planning. To train TrackVLA, the authors created a new Embodied Visual Tracking Benchmark (EVT-Bench) containing 1.7 million samples with diverse difficulty levels.  The paper reports state-of-the-art performance on public benchmarks (Gym-UnrealCV) and the EVT-Bench, demonstrating strong generalizability and robustness to occlusion and high dynamics in both simulated and real-world scenarios.  The model achieves a 10 FPS inference speed in real-world settings. The authors make their code and the EVT-Bench dataset publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   **Integration:** The core novelty lies in the *integrated* approach to object recognition and trajectory planning within a VLA model. Most prior methods treat these as separate modules, which can lead to error accumulation.  The unified architecture with shared LLM backbone and task-specific heads (LM for recognition, diffusion for planning) is a significant design choice that promotes synergy.
    *   **Anchor-based Diffusion:** The use of an anchor-based diffusion model for trajectory generation is another novel aspect. It provides a way to guide the diffusion process with pre-defined trajectory patterns, improving efficiency and stability compared to vanilla diffusion policies. This is a particularly important contribution given the computational cost typically associated with diffusion models.
    *   **EVT-Bench Dataset:**  The creation of a large-scale, diverse embodied visual tracking benchmark (EVT-Bench) is a valuable contribution to the community.  The varying difficulty levels and the inclusion of detailed language inputs address limitations of existing datasets focused on simpler scenarios or category-level tracking.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Performance Gains:** The paper demonstrates significant performance improvements over existing methods on both synthetic and real-world benchmarks.  The zero-shot transfer results on Gym-UnrealCV, as well as the strong results on the new EVT-Bench, indicate the model's generalization ability.
    *   **Real-world Applicability:** The reported 10 FPS inference speed in real-world settings is crucial for practical deployment in robotics applications. This makes the method useful in real-world situations.
    *   **Community Impact:** The public release of the code and the EVT-Bench dataset will facilitate further research and development in embodied visual tracking and VLA models. The EVT-Bench can become a valuable resource for comparing different approaches and evaluating progress in the field.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined Problem:** The paper addresses a challenging and important problem in embodied AI.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The evaluation is thorough, with comparisons to multiple baselines and experiments in both synthetic and real-world environments. The different difficulty levels on the EVT-Bench are well-defined.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the model architecture, training procedure, and experimental results.
    *   **Practicality:** The real-time performance and real-world experiments demonstrate the practical potential of the method.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limitations in Real-world:** The limitations section indicates that it relies on egocentric observation and limited motion control. This limits real-world utility for general robotic applications.
    *   **Dataset Bias?** While EVT-Bench is comprehensive, all the robots and humans were in simulation and, as such, might not capture all the nuances of the real world (e.g., lighting, textures).
    *   **Lack of Theoretical Justification:** While the empirical results are strong, the paper lacks a strong theoretical analysis of why the integrated approach is more effective than modular approaches. It would be helpful to provide some insight into the underlying mechanisms that contribute to the synergy between recognition and planning.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The integrated VLA approach is likely to inspire further research on end-to-end learning for embodied tasks.
    *   The EVT-Bench dataset will become a standard benchmark for evaluating embodied visual tracking algorithms.
    *   The anchor-based diffusion model may find broader application in other robotic tasks requiring trajectory generation.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of embodied AI, demonstrating the benefits of a unified VLA approach for visual tracking. The anchor-based diffusion and the EVT-Bench dataset are novel and valuable contributions. The strong experimental results and real-time performance highlight the practical potential of the method. While there are some limitations and the lack of deep theoretical analysis, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses.
Score: 7
