### MemAscend: System Memory Optimization for SSD-Offloaded LLM Fine-Tuning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23254v1)
- **Authors**: Yong-Cheng Liaw, Shuo-Han Chen
- **Abstract**: Owing to the huge success of generative artificial intelligence (AI), large language models (LLMs) have emerged as a core subclass, underpinning applications such as question answering, text generation, and code completion. While fine-tuning these models on domain-specific data can yield significant performance gains, it also poses daunting computational challenges, especially for researchers and small organizations with limited hardware resources. Although SSD offloading (i.e., ZeRO-Infinity) has emerged as a viable strategy to overcome the GPU memory barrier via leveraging both system memory (i.e., CPU DRAM) and storage space (i.e., solid-state devices, SSDs), its design primarily targets model-centric performance issues. As a result, key system-level issues, including system memory fragmentation, inefficient pinned buffer allocation, peak CPU usage spikes, and file system overhead, remain unaddressed, stifling scalability and inflating costs. Such an observation motivates this paper to introduce MemAscend, a framework that systematically tackles the underexplored system memory bottlenecks in SSD-offloaded LLM training, with a focus on resource-constrained environments. By streamlining pinned-memory allocation, eradicating fragmentation, and mitigating peak overhead, MemAscend reclaims a substantial system memory budget, enabling larger models, longer context windows, and higher batch sizes without exceeding modest hardware limits. Across diverse LLM benchmarks, MemAscend reduces peak system-memory consumption by an average of 55.7% compared with standard SSD offloading techniques, lowering the hardware barrier for fine-tuning and unlocking new possibilities for cost-effective large-scale training on limited-resource machines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MemAscend, a framework for optimizing system memory usage during SSD-offloaded fine-tuning of Large Language Models (LLMs) in resource-constrained environments. It addresses system-level inefficiencies, like memory fragmentation, inefficient pinned buffer allocation, peak CPU usage during overflow checks, and filesystem overhead, that are often overlooked in existing SSD offloading techniques such as ZeRO-Infinity. MemAscend incorporates an adaptive buffer pool, custom memory allocator, fused overflow check kernel, and a direct NVMe engine to reclaim memory and improve I/O throughput. The results show reduced peak memory consumption, enabling larger models, longer context windows, and higher batch sizes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the **systematic focus on system memory bottlenecks within the context of SSD-offloaded LLM fine-tuning**. While techniques like ZeRO-Infinity address GPU memory limitations, MemAscend tackles the often-overlooked inefficiencies in how system memory is utilized, particularly in resource-limited scenarios. The individual components, like adaptive buffer pools and fused kernels, are not entirely new concepts in isolation, but their **combination and specific application to the SSD-offloaded LLM fine-tuning pipeline represent a novel contribution**. A more in-depth comparison with other system memory management techniques would strengthen the novelty claim.

*   **Significance:** The work is significant because it **lowers the hardware barrier for fine-tuning large models**. By optimizing system memory, MemAscend makes it feasible to fine-tune LLMs on machines with limited resources (smaller GPU clusters or single GPUs with modest system memory), expanding accessibility to a broader range of researchers and practitioners. The reported memory reductions and throughput improvements are substantial and practically meaningful. The potential impact on democratizing access to LLM fine-tuning is a key strength. The results presented offer an alternative solution to reduce the cost of training and fine-tuning LLMs for smaller research and academic organizations.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Problem Focus:** It addresses a practically relevant and often-overlooked problem in LLM fine-tuning.
    *   **Systematic Approach:** The framework tackles multiple system-level bottlenecks in a cohesive manner.
    *   **Empirical Evaluation:**  The experiments are comprehensive, covering a range of model sizes, context lengths, and hardware configurations. The comparison with ZeRO-Infinity provides a strong baseline.
    *   **Practical Impact:** The results demonstrate tangible improvements in memory usage, throughput, and the ability to train larger models/longer sequences on resource-constrained hardware.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Component Novelty:** While the combination is novel, some individual components are adaptations of existing techniques. More detail regarding the novelty in combining the elements and its advantage would make a strong claim.
    *   **Limited Benchmarking Scope:** The models used for benchmarking, while relevant, could be expanded to include a wider range of architectures or those more representative of real-world fine-tuning tasks.
    *   **System Specifics:** The details regarding the system configurations and software used in Section II may need updating to match the benchmarks with Qwen2.5 models.
    *   **Generalization:** While the study focuses on SSD offloading, it is not completely clear whether this system is transferrable to other heterogenous memory systems, such as persistent memory.

*   **Justification for Score:**

    While the paper does not introduce radically new individual algorithms, its **systematic approach to optimizing system memory in the specific context of SSD-offloaded LLM fine-tuning constitutes a solid contribution**. The empirical results are convincing, and the potential for enabling LLM fine-tuning on more accessible hardware is significant. Given that the novelty lies in the integration and application of known techniques to a specific problem rather than fundamental algorithmic breakthroughs, a score in the upper-middle range is justified.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.DC
- **Score**: 7/10

### Can Large Language Models Trigger a Paradigm Shift in Travel Behavior Modeling? Experiences with Modeling Travel Satisfaction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23262v1)
- **Authors**: Pengfei Xu, Donggen Wang
- **Abstract**: As a specific domain of subjective well-being, travel satisfaction has attracted much research attention recently. Previous studies primarily use statistical models and, more recently, machine learning models to explore the determinants of travel satisfaction. Both approaches require data from sufficient sample sizes and correct prior statistical assumptions. The emergence of Large Language Models (LLMs) offers a new modeling approach that can overcome the shortcomings of the existing methods. Pre-trained on extensive datasets, LLMs have strong capabilities in contextual understanding and generalization, significantly reducing their dependence on large quantities of task-specific data and stringent statistical assumptions. The primary challenge in applying LLMs lies in addressing the behavioral misalignment between LLMs and human behavior. Using data on travel satisfaction from a household survey in shanghai, this study identifies the existence and source of misalignment and develop methods to address the misalignment issue. We find that the zero-shot LLM exhibits behavioral misalignment, resulting in relatively low prediction accuracy. However, few-shot learning, even with a limited number of samples, allows the model to outperform baseline models in MSE and MAPE metrics. This misalignment can be attributed to the gap between the general knowledge embedded in LLMs and the specific, unique characteristics of the dataset. On these bases, we propose an LLM-based modeling approach that can be applied to model travel behavior using samples of small sizes. This study highlights the potential of LLMs for modeling not only travel satisfaction but also broader aspects of travel behavior.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the application of Large Language Models (LLMs) in travel behavior modeling, specifically focusing on predicting travel satisfaction. The authors address the issue of "behavioral misalignment" between LLMs and human travelers, where LLMs' outputs and reasoning are inconsistent with actual human behavior. The study uses data from a household travel survey in Shanghai to identify the source of this misalignment and develop methods to improve it. The authors find that a zero-shot LLM (without specific training data) exhibits misalignment, leading to lower prediction accuracy. However, few-shot learning, using even a limited number of samples, allows the LLM to outperform baseline statistical (Linear Regression) and machine learning (GBDT) models. The paper analyzes variable importance and reasoning processes to understand the impact of few-shot learning in aligning the LLM with human behavior. Ultimately, it proposes an LLM-based modeling approach that can be applied to travel behavior modeling with small sample sizes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a crucial and relatively unexplored challenge in applying LLMs to behavioral modeling: behavioral misalignment. The explicit identification and analysis of misalignment in the context of travel satisfaction is a valuable contribution. It goes beyond simple application and delves into the fundamental issues.
    *   **Methodology:** The use of both zero-shot and few-shot learning, combined with comparison to established methods like Linear Regression and GBDT, provides a robust framework for evaluating LLM performance. The investigation of variable importance and reasoning process provides crucial insight into the source of this behavioral misalignment.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The finding that few-shot learning can significantly improve LLM performance, even with small sample sizes, has important practical implications. It suggests a pathway for reducing the costs associated with data collection in travel behavior research and enabling more agile and responsive modeling approaches.
    *   **Clear Articulation of Limitations:** The paper acknowledges several limitations, including the potential for hallucination, high cost, and inefficiency associated with LLMs, demonstrating a balanced and realistic perspective.
    *   **Generalizable Findings:** The conclusion that LLMs have unique strengths in understanding the intersection of textual data from news articles and social media posts with traditional quantitative survey data is a key finding for future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Data Specificity:** The analysis is based on a single dataset from Shanghai, which limits the generalizability of the findings. While the approach is sound, the specific conclusions about variable importance might vary across different cultural contexts or geographical locations.
    *   **Limited Scope of Alignment Methods:** While few-shot learning proves effective, the paper could benefit from exploring other techniques for aligning LLMs with human behavior, such as reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF) or more sophisticated prompt engineering strategies. The analysis could be extended by employing several other techniques to deal with behavioral misalignment.
    *   **Hallucinations and Repetitive Patterns are not Fully Addressed:** While the paper acknowledges the limitations of the 'parroting problem', there is no concrete plan on how these limitations would be overcome with the identified mitigation strategy. The conclusions are therefore, limited in their feasibility.
    *   **Over-emphasis on Few-Shot:** The success of few-shot learning is partially attributed to the LLM's extensive prior knowledge. It becomes important to delineate between the effectiveness of few-shot learning and pre-existing models.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper's significance lies in its critical assessment of LLMs in a specific domain of behavioral research.
    *   The explicit identification of behavioral misalignment provides a framework for future research.
    *   The proposed LLM-based modeling approach offers a potentially transformative approach to travel behavior modeling.

**Justification:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field by critically examining the application of LLMs in travel behavior modeling. It addresses the critical issue of behavioral misalignment with sound methodology and a clear analysis of results, including the limitations.
However, the reliance on a single dataset and limited exploration of other alignment methods restricts the generalizability of the study. Also, the over-emphasis on few-shot learning to be a primary means to overcome behavioral misalignment limits its scope. Overall, the paper offers a thought-provoking analysis with important practical implications, but its scope needs to be expanded.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CY
- **Score**: 7/10

### Efficiently Access Diffusion Fisher: Within the Outer Product Span Space
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23264v1)
- **Authors**: Fangyikang Wang, Hubery Yin, Shaobin Zhuang, Huminhao Zhu, Yinan Li, Lei Qian, Chao Zhang, Hanbin Zhao, Hui Qian, Chen Li
- **Abstract**: Recent Diffusion models (DMs) advancements have explored incorporating the second-order diffusion Fisher information (DF), defined as the negative Hessian of log density, into various downstream tasks and theoretical analysis. However, current practices typically approximate the diffusion Fisher by applying auto-differentiation to the learned score network. This black-box method, though straightforward, lacks any accuracy guarantee and is time-consuming. In this paper, we show that the diffusion Fisher actually resides within a space spanned by the outer products of score and initial data. Based on the outer-product structure, we develop two efficient approximation algorithms to access the trace and matrix-vector multiplication of DF, respectively. These algorithms bypass the auto-differentiation operations with time-efficient vector-product calculations. Furthermore, we establish the approximation error bounds for the proposed algorithms. Experiments in likelihood evaluation and adjoint optimization demonstrate the superior accuracy and reduced computational cost of our proposed algorithms. Additionally, based on the novel outer-product formulation of DF, we design the first numerical verification experiment for the optimal transport property of the general PF-ODE deduced map.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the computationally expensive task of accessing the diffusion Fisher information (DF) in diffusion models (DMs).  Instead of relying on auto-differentiation of the score network, the authors demonstrate that the DF resides within a space spanned by the outer products of the score and the initial data. Based on this insight, they develop two efficient approximation algorithms to access the trace and matrix-vector multiplication of the DF, avoiding auto-differentiation and reducing computational cost.  The algorithms are supported by approximation error bounds.  The paper also presents a numerical verification experiment for the optimal transport property of the general PF-ODE deduced map.  Experiments on likelihood evaluation and adjoint optimization demonstrate improved accuracy and reduced computational cost compared to traditional methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the analytical formulation of the diffusion Fisher information as a weighted sum of outer products, which enables computationally efficient approximations. Discovering this low-rank structure is a significant contribution. The two algorithms developed for accessing trace and matrix-vector products are also novel. The use of this new formulation to numerically test the OT properties of diffusion-ODE maps is another novel element.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical bottleneck in utilizing the DF, which is a potentially useful component for downstream tasks such as likelihood evaluation, adjoint optimization, and optimal transport analysis. By significantly reducing the computational cost of accessing the DF, this paper could enable wider application of DF in these contexts. It could also impact theoretical analysis of DMs by offering a better way to use second-order information. The numerical verification for the OT property is also potentially significant.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper provides a strong theoretical foundation for its algorithms.
    *   The algorithms are well-motivated and address a clear problem.
    *   The paper provides approximation error bounds for their algorithms.
    *   The experiments show a clear improvement in accuracy and speed compared to the VJP baseline.
    *   Numerical evidence is presented that aligns with current theories on the optimal transport properties of certain diffusion models, while also offering new insights for more general setups.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The initial derivation is performed under the restrictive assumption of a Dirac mixture for the initial distribution, though it is later generalized. However, this simplifying assumption might not be applicable to all types of data and DMs.
    *   While the empirical results are convincing, more extensive experiments across a wider range of datasets and DM architectures could strengthen the claims.
    *   The practical applicability of the DF still needs to be fully demonstrated. The downstream tasks showcased in the paper, while relevant, do not fully exploit the potential of the DF as a second-order signal in DMs.
    *   The numerical verification of the optimal transport property, while a significant contribution, could be extended to a larger set of initial conditions and noise schedules to provide more robust evidence.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to become a foundational work for efficient computation and applications of diffusion Fisher information. If further work validates and expands upon these findings, this paper could have a substantial impact. The paper can provide a path towards a better understanding of the theoretical properties of DMs.

*   **Justification for Score:** The analytical derivation and two proposed algorithms, in conjunction with numerical validation of OT properties, demonstrates a clear and novel contribution. The paper addresses a recognized inefficiency in the DM field with a method that is both theoretically grounded and practically beneficial. However, the paper is still focused on a somewhat niche problem (efficient DF access) and doesn't immediately revolutionize the state-of-the-art in generative modelling. The limitations regarding restrictive assumptions, experimental setup, and practical applicability are taken into account when awarding the score.

- **Classification**: cs.LG

### Image Aesthetic Reasoning: A New Benchmark for Medical Image Screening with MLLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23265v1)
- **Authors**: Zheng Sun, Yi Wei, Long Yu
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) are of great application across many domains, such as multimodal understanding and generation. With the development of diffusion models (DM) and unified MLLMs, the performance of image generation has been significantly improved, however, the study of image screening is rare and its performance with MLLMs is unsatisfactory due to the lack of data and the week image aesthetic reasoning ability in MLLMs. In this work, we propose a complete solution to address these problems in terms of data and methodology. For data, we collect a comprehensive medical image screening dataset with 1500+ samples, each sample consists of a medical image, four generated images, and a multiple-choice answer. The dataset evaluates the aesthetic reasoning ability under four aspects: \textit{(1) Appearance Deformation, (2) Principles of Physical Lighting and Shadow, (3) Placement Layout, (4) Extension Rationality}. For methodology, we utilize long chains of thought (CoT) and Group Relative Policy Optimization with Dynamic Proportional Accuracy reward, called DPA-GRPO, to enhance the image aesthetic reasoning ability of MLLMs. Our experimental results reveal that even state-of-the-art closed-source MLLMs, such as GPT-4o and Qwen-VL-Max, exhibit performance akin to random guessing in image aesthetic reasoning. In contrast, by leveraging the reinforcement learning approach, we are able to surpass the score of both large-scale models and leading closed-source models using a much smaller model. We hope our attempt on medical image screening will serve as a regular configuration in image aesthetic reasoning in the future.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new benchmark for evaluating the image aesthetic reasoning capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs), specifically in the context of medical image screening. The authors address the limitations in current MLLM performance in this area by proposing a comprehensive solution that includes a novel medical image screening dataset and a reinforcement learning-based methodology. The dataset contains medical images, generated variations with aesthetic flaws, and multiple-choice questions to evaluate the MLLMs' ability to identify these flaws. The authors also present DPA-GRPO, a two-stage reinforcement learning approach based on Chain-of-Thought (CoT) and Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) with a Dynamic Proportional Accuracy reward, to enhance the image aesthetic reasoning ability of smaller MLLMs. Experimental results demonstrate that even state-of-the-art closed-source MLLMs perform poorly on the benchmark, while their DPA-GRPO approach significantly improves performance and surpasses the capabilities of larger models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates novelty in several aspects:

    *   **New Dataset:** Creating a specific medical image screening dataset focused on aesthetic reasoning is a valuable contribution. Existing datasets often lack the targeted flaws and the medical domain-specific nuances, making this dataset a significant advancement. This is especially crucial given that existing data sources often have limited coverage on medical imaging and aesthetic reasoning tasks.
    *   **DPA-GRPO Method:** The use of a dynamic proportional accuracy reward with GRPO in the context of aesthetic reasoning, particularly in the medical domain, is novel. While GRPO itself isn't new, the adaptation and combination with a novel reward structure tailored to the multiple-choice nature of the task is a significant improvement.
    *   **Problem Formulation:** Framing image screening in medical context in terms of aesthetic reasoning highlights an often overlooked issue.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical gap in MLLM capabilities for a high-stakes application: medical image screening. This work could have significant implications for automating and improving the accuracy of medical image analysis. By focusing on aesthetic reasoning, the paper highlights the importance of understanding subtle flaws and variations in generated medical images, which directly impacts the reliability of AI-assisted diagnostic tools.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the problem of image aesthetic reasoning in medical image screening and identifies the limitations of existing MLLMs.
    *   **Comprehensive Solution:** The authors propose a complete solution that includes a new dataset, a novel methodology, and thorough experimental evaluation.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of their DPA-GRPO approach, showing significant improvements over baseline methods and state-of-the-art models.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The paper details the dataset construction process, methodology, and experimental setup, enhancing the potential for reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dataset Size:** While a valuable contribution, the dataset could benefit from being larger and having more diverse image types to improve the generalizability of the models trained on it. A dataset size of only 1500+ samples is considered rather modest.
    *   **Limited Focus:** The paper primarily concentrates on generated medical images. An extension to real-world medical images with naturally occurring flaws would increase the real-world applicability of the study.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** While the ablation studies shed light on the effects of different reward methods, they could have included a deeper examination of parameters affecting performance of GRPO itself.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more robust and reliable MLLMs for medical image analysis. The new dataset and the DPA-GRPO approach provide a valuable resource for researchers in the field. The paper also highlights the importance of considering aesthetic reasoning in the development of AI-assisted diagnostic tools.

**Justification of Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses of the paper, a score of **8** is justified. The creation of a new, specialized dataset coupled with the development and empirical validation of a novel reinforcement learning methodology for enhancing image aesthetic reasoning in MLLMs represents a significant contribution. While limitations exist regarding dataset size and a lack of ablation, the paper provides a clear advancement in an important area and has the potential to significantly impact the future direction of MLLM research in medical image analysis. Specifically, the paper highlights a gap in current modeling approaches and provides a strong foundation for future work.
