The concept of categorizing LLMs based on their semantic understanding capabilities and then tailoring the attack strategy is interesting. The combination of prompt mutation and multi-layered encryption, particularly the use of binary tree encryption and Caesar cipher, isn't entirely new, *but* the orchestrated way they are used to target the varying comprehension capabilities of different LLMs does introduce a degree of innovation.  Prior work often focuses on a single attack method applicable (or attempted) across various LLMs, whereas this paper attempts a tiered approach.
*   **Significance:**  The paper's significance lies in its practical demonstration of LLM vulnerability and the need for adaptive defense mechanisms. If the reported 98.9% success rate against a cutting-edge model like GPT-4o is accurate and consistently reproducible, that would be a significant (and concerning) finding. It highlights that even the most advanced models still have inherent weaknesses that can be exploited by well-designed adversarial attacks. The analysis of how the method bypasses various layers of LLM security (input filtering, inference compliance, output moderation) contributes to a better understanding of LLM security limitations.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the problem of LLM jailbreaking and the limitations of existing techniques.
    *   **Adaptive Approach:** The adaptive strategy based on semantic understanding is a good concept.
    *   **Multi-layered Obfuscation:** Combining prompt mutation and multi-layered encryption is reasonable.
    *   **Empirical Evaluation:** The paper presents experimental results comparing the proposed method to several baselines.
    *   **Ablation study**: The inclusion of an ablation study helps demonstrate the importance of the different components.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Details on 'Fu' Function:** The description of the programmatically generated function 'Fu' is somewhat vague. Providing more details about how this function is generated and how it achieves prompt mutation would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Reproducibility Concerns:**  While results are provided, the inherent difficulty in reproducing attacks against actively evolving LLMs is a major challenge.  The models used are specific versions with timestamps, making independent verification difficult over time. Releasing code would somewhat mitigate this, but even then, the models themselves will inevitably change.
    *   **Overstated Claims:**  The 98.9% success rate is suspiciously high. It's plausible but warrants extreme caution. The evaluation metric relies on using another LLM (Kimi moonshot-v1-8k) as an evaluator, which itself is not a perfect measure, and may be biased. A broader set of evaluators would strengthen confidence.
    *   **Limited Generalizability:** While the paper categorizes LLMs into two types, the applicability of these strategies beyond the specific LLMs tested is uncertain. The method might be highly dependent on the specific architectures and training data of the target models.  It is also unclear whether the same attack would succeed multiple times on the same model, or whether the jailbreaking attacks are easily thwarted through minor changes in the model or security measures.
    *   **Ethical Considerations:** The ethical implications of publishing effective jailbreaking techniques should be carefully considered, although the authors do not make any specific discussion. The information can be misused to generate harmful content.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research in LLM security by highlighting the importance of adaptive defense mechanisms and the need to account for the semantic understanding capabilities of LLMs. The specific techniques proposed (prompt mutation, multi-layered encryption) could serve as building blocks for more sophisticated attacks and defenses.
    *   It also has importance in guiding the responsible development of the large language models.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper has *some* novelty in its adaptive approach and demonstrates a solid understanding of LLM vulnerabilities. The experimental results, if verifiable, point to a concerning level of success in bypassing LLM safety mechanisms. However, the limited details on the 'Fu' function, reproducibility concerns, potential for overstated claims and generalizability limitations, and a lack of ethical consideration detract from its overall impact.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### From Parameters to Prompts: Understanding and Mitigating the Factuality Gap between Fine-Tuned LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23410v1)
- **Authors**: Xuan Gong, Hanbo Huang, Shiyu Liang
- **Abstract**: Factual knowledge extraction aims to explicitly extract knowledge parameterized in pre-trained language models for application in downstream tasks. While prior work has been investigating the impact of supervised fine-tuning data on the factuality of large language models (LLMs), its mechanism remains poorly understood. We revisit this impact through systematic experiments, with a particular focus on the factuality gap that arises when fine-tuning on known versus unknown knowledge. Our findings show that this gap can be mitigated at the inference stage, either under out-of-distribution (OOD) settings or by using appropriate in-context learning (ICL) prompts (i.e., few-shot learning and Chain of Thought (CoT)). We prove this phenomenon theoretically from the perspective of knowledge graphs, showing that the test-time prompt may diminish or even overshadow the impact of fine-tuning data and play a dominant role in knowledge extraction. Ultimately, our results shed light on the interaction between finetuning data and test-time prompt, demonstrating that ICL can effectively compensate for shortcomings in fine-tuning data, and highlighting the need to reconsider the use of ICL prompting as a means to evaluate the effectiveness of fine-tuning data selection methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the factuality gap that arises in large language models (LLMs) when fine-tuned on known versus unknown knowledge. Through experiments, they show that this gap can be mitigated during inference using techniques like out-of-distribution (OOD) settings or in-context learning (ICL) prompts, especially few-shot learning and Chain-of-Thought (CoT). They provide a theoretical explanation based on knowledge graphs, arguing that test-time prompts can reduce the impact of fine-tuning data, potentially overshadowing its influence on knowledge extraction.  Their results demonstrate ICL can compensate for shortcomings in fine-tuning data, suggesting that ICL prompting as a means to evaluate the effectiveness of fine-tuning data selection methods needs to be reconsidered.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of a factuality gap based on training data familiarity is not entirely new, as previous work has explored the impact of fine-tuning data on LLM factuality. However, the paper's novel contribution lies in:
    *   Systematically investigating the interplay *between* fine-tuning data (known vs. unknown) and inference-time prompting strategies.
    *   Providing a theoretical framework (knowledge graph-based) to explain the observed phenomenon and the mitigation strategies using ICL.
    *   Highlighting the implications for evaluating fine-tuning data selection methods.

*   **Significance:** The findings are significant because they challenge the common practice of evaluating fine-tuned models solely based on their performance without considering the impact of inference-time prompting. It demonstrates that ICL can compensate for lower-quality fine-tuning data, raising questions about how we assess the true value of data selection techniques. The knowledge graph-based theoretical explanation provides a valuable framework for understanding and potentially predicting the behavior of LLMs in terms of factual knowledge. The paper could influence how researchers design and evaluate fine-tuning strategies and data selection algorithms.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive experimental setup covering various models, tasks, and prompting strategies.
    *   Solid theoretical grounding using a knowledge graph perspective.
    *   Clear and well-defined research questions.
    *   Practical implications for evaluating data selection methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The theoretical framework, while novel in its application to this specific problem, relies on simplified assumptions (e.g., one-layer transformer). This might limit its generalizability to more complex LLM architectures.
    *   The empirical results, while compelling, could benefit from more diverse datasets and tasks to further strengthen the conclusions.
    *   The analysis of MMLU and WikiBios is less conclusive, suggesting some limitations in the framework or that additional factors are at play in those datasets.
    * The analysis relies on external embedding models which might not perfectly align with LLM's internal representation of knowledge.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence the design and evaluation of fine-tuning data selection methods. By highlighting the role of ICL in mitigating factuality gaps, it encourages a more holistic approach that considers the interaction between fine-tuning and inference-time prompting strategies. It also provides a new theoretical lens (knowledge graph modeling) for understanding and improving the factual knowledge of LLMs.

**Justification of Score:**

While the general problem area has been explored previously, the paper's systematic investigation of the *interaction* between fine-tuning data and inference-time prompts, coupled with a knowledge graph-based theoretical explanation, represents a valuable contribution. The practical implications for evaluating data selection methods are also significant. The score is tempered by the simplified assumptions in the theoretical framework and the need for further validation with more diverse datasets.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### KVzip: Query-Agnostic KV Cache Compression with Context Reconstruction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23416v1)
- **Authors**: Jang-Hyun Kim, Jinuk Kim, Sangwoo Kwon, Jae W. Lee, Sangdoo Yun, Hyun Oh Song
- **Abstract**: Transformer-based large language models (LLMs) cache context as key-value (KV) pairs during inference. As context length grows, KV cache sizes expand, leading to substantial memory overhead and increased attention latency. This paper introduces KVzip, a query-agnostic KV cache eviction method enabling effective reuse of compressed KV caches across diverse queries. KVzip quantifies the importance of a KV pair using the underlying LLM to reconstruct original contexts from cached KV pairs, subsequently evicting pairs with lower importance. Extensive empirical evaluations demonstrate that KVzip reduces KV cache size by 3-4$\times$ and FlashAttention decoding latency by approximately 2$\times$, with negligible performance loss in question-answering, retrieval, reasoning, and code comprehension tasks. Evaluations include various models such as LLaMA3.1-8B, Qwen2.5-14B, and Gemma3-12B, with context lengths reaching up to 170K tokens. KVzip significantly outperforms existing query-aware KV eviction methods, which suffer from performance degradation even at a 90% cache budget ratio under multi-query scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces KVzip, a novel query-agnostic method for compressing the key-value (KV) cache in transformer-based Large Language Models (LLMs) during inference. KVzip aims to address the memory overhead and latency issues associated with large KV caches, particularly when dealing with long contexts and multiple queries. The core idea is to identify and evict less important KV pairs based on their contribution to reconstructing the original context. The method utilizes the LLM itself as an encoder-decoder to assess KV-pair importance. The experiments show that KVzip achieves significant KV cache size reduction and reduced FlashAttention decoding latency, with minimal performance loss across various tasks and models, outperforming query-aware eviction methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the query-agnostic approach to KV cache compression. Unlike existing methods that rely on query-specific information to determine which KV pairs to retain, KVzip focuses on retaining information crucial for reconstructing the original context. The use of the LLM as an encoder-decoder for determining KV pair importance is a clever and potentially impactful technique. The approach of using a reconstruction objective is not entirely new in self-supervised learning, but its application to KV cache compression in this way is a significant contribution.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical problem in deploying LLMs, especially those with long-context capabilities: memory footprint and inference latency. The ability to compress KV caches significantly without sacrificing performance is a key enabler for efficient and cost-effective LLM deployment. The demonstrations of improved latency and reduced memory usage are compelling. The advantage of KVzip being query-agnostic is also significant, as it allows for preparing the cache offline and reusing it across many queries, which is beneficial for scenarios like chatbots or document retrieval systems. The integration with existing optimizations like KV cache quantization further increases the practical impact.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The core idea of query-agnostic compression based on context reconstruction is novel and well-motivated.
    *   The empirical evaluation is comprehensive, covering various models, datasets, and tasks.
    *   The results show significant improvements in KV cache size and latency reduction with minimal performance degradation.
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the method and its advantages.
    *   The method appears easily integrable with existing optimizations like quantization.
    *   The provided analysis of attention sparsity is insightful and offers a solid understanding of the method’s efficacy.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The computational overhead of importance scoring is acknowledged, adding approximately twice the causal-attention complexity to the prefill stage. While the authors address this with chunked scoring, further optimization could be beneficial.
    *   The "necessity of context reconstruction" analysis in the paper suggests that reconstructing the full context is important, which might add to the computation during the scoring phase. More analyses on other forms of scoring inputs might be beneficial.
    *   The softmax-free importance scoring section showed some degradation in compression ratios.
    *   The paper provides limited discussion about the robustness of the compression strategy to adversarial examples or noisy input contexts. Does the reconstruction-based approach inherently make the KV cache more vulnerable or resilient?
*   **Potential Impact:** KVzip has the potential to significantly impact the deployment of LLMs by making them more efficient and cost-effective. The query-agnostic nature of the compression makes it suitable for various real-world applications. The insights regarding attention sparsity and context reconstruction could also inspire further research in this area. The demonstrated efficiency also makes it more environmentally conscious. The method's ability to integrate with other methods is another strength.

**Justification:**

KVzip's novel approach and significant practical benefits justify a relatively high score. While there is overhead in the scoring and chunking process, the extensive empirical results show that this overhead is well worth it, and there is further opportunity for optimization. The query-agnostic nature and compatibility with other techniques are substantial advantages. The method effectively balances the trade-off between compression and performance across diverse tasks and models.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.DB
- **Score**: 8/10

### SWE-bench Goes Live!
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23419v1)
- **Authors**: Linghao Zhang, Shilin He, Chaoyun Zhang, Yu Kang, Bowen Li, Chengxing Xie, Junhao Wang, Maoquan Wang, Yufan Huang, Shengyu Fu, Elsie Nallipogu, Qingwei Lin, Yingnong Dang, Saravan Rajmohan, Dongmei Zhang
- **Abstract**: The issue-resolving task, where a model generates patches to fix real-world bugs, has emerged as a critical benchmark for evaluating the capabilities of large language models (LLMs). While SWE-bench and its variants have become standard in this domain, they suffer from key limitations: they have not been updated since their initial releases, cover a narrow set of repositories, and depend heavily on manual effort for instance construction and environment setup. These factors hinder scalability and introduce risks of overfitting and data contamination. In this work, we present \textbf{SWE-bench-Live}, a \textit{live-updatable} benchmark designed to overcome these challenges. Our initial release consists of 1,319 tasks derived from real GitHub issues created since 2024, spanning 93 repositories. Each task is accompanied by a dedicated Docker image to ensure reproducible execution. Central to our benchmark is \method, an automated curation pipeline that streamlines the entire process from instance creation to environment setup, removing manual bottlenecks and enabling scalability and continuous updates. We evaluate a range of state-of-the-art agent frameworks and LLMs on SWE-bench-Live, revealing a substantial performance gap compared to static benchmarks like SWE-bench, even under controlled evaluation conditions. To better understand this discrepancy, we perform detailed analyses across repository origin, issue recency, and task difficulty. By providing a fresh, diverse, and executable benchmark grounded in live repository activity, SWE-bench-Live facilitates rigorous, contamination-resistant evaluation of LLMs and agents in dynamic, real-world software development settings.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SWE-bench Goes Live!":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SWE-bench-Live, a dynamically updated benchmark for evaluating the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) and code agents to resolve real-world software bugs.  SWE-bench-Live addresses limitations of existing benchmarks like SWE-bench, which are static, have limited repository coverage, and rely heavily on manual curation. SWE-bench-Live features: (1) a continuous update mechanism leveraging the REPOLAUNCH pipeline that automates instance creation and environment setup; (2) broader repository coverage, currently including 1,319 tasks from 93 repositories based on GitHub issues created since 2024; (3) a Dockerized environment for each task, ensuring reproducibility; and (4) focus on issue resolution requiring multi-file reasoning and environment interaction. The paper evaluates several agent frameworks and LLMs on the benchmark, revealing a performance gap compared to static benchmarks and demonstrating the challenges of generalizing to unseen, dynamically arriving bugs. Finally, they performed a failure mode analysis of LLMs on SWE-bench-Live, breaking it down across repository origin, issue recency, and task difficulty.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty of the paper lies in the *dynamic and automated nature* of the benchmark. While the task itself (issue resolution) is established, continuously updating the benchmark and automating instance creation via REPOLAUNCH is a significant advancement.  This addresses the crucial issue of data contamination that plagues LLM evaluation. Prior 'live' benchmarks exist, but focus on algorithmic tasks, not the more complex setting of real-world repository-level bugs that SWE-bench-Live tackles. Another key novelty is identifying the weakness of LLMs for fixing bugs involving multiple files or a large scope.

*   **Significance:**  SWE-bench-Live has the potential to significantly improve LLM and code agent evaluation. The dynamic nature of the benchmark forces models to generalize to unseen issues, providing a more realistic measure of their capabilities. Broader repository coverage allows evaluating model performance across various codebases and programming styles. The paper makes a solid first step and makes the important observation about LLMs not generalizing well to the multi-file editing scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a key problem:** Data contamination is a severe issue in LLM evaluation, and SWE-bench-Live provides a means to mitigate this risk.
    *   **Automation:** REPOLAUNCH is a valuable contribution in itself, as it reduces the manual effort required to create and maintain benchmarks of this type.
    *   **Comprehensive analysis:** The initial evaluation and error analysis provide valuable insights into the challenges of real-world bug fixing.
    *   **Clear evaluation:** The use of readily understandable metric - Resolved Rate - improves readability and interpretability.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Python Focus:** The initial release focuses solely on Python repositories. While Python is widely used, expanding to other languages is crucial for broader applicability.
    *   **Reliance on test suite:** The benchmark depends on the existence and quality of existing test suites. Bugs in test suites or lack of comprehensive test coverage can affect the evaluation accuracy. This is not a problem of their benchmark, but inherent to any benchmark grounded in real-world test suites.
    *   **Reproducibility of LLM calls:** The LLM calls are made using official APIs; however, the experiments might lead to potential randomness.
    *   **Limited Recency Window:** While the benchmark includes issues from the previous year, older issues might still be included in model training data.

*   **Potential Influence:**  SWE-bench-Live is likely to become a valuable resource for the software engineering and LLM research community.  It can drive the development of more robust and generalizable code agents. The dynamic nature of the benchmark will encourage researchers to focus on continuous improvement rather than overfitting to a fixed dataset.

*   **Justification of Score:** The paper presents a significant contribution by addressing a crucial problem (data contamination) with a novel approach (dynamic benchmarking). REPOLAUNCH is a useful tool, and the initial evaluation is insightful. However, the limitations in language support and test suite dependence temper its overall impact. The paper presents a compelling approach to a practical problem, making the benchmark quite helpful, but it still has room for growth to broaden scope and generalizability.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Enhanced DACER Algorithm with High Diffusion Efficiency
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23426v1)
- **Authors**: Yinuo Wang, Mining Tan, Wenjun Zou, Haotian Lin, Xujie Song, Wenxuan Wang, Tong Liu, Likun Wang, Guojian Zhan, Tianze Zhu, Shiqi Liu, Jingliang Duan, Shengbo Eben Li
- **Abstract**: Due to their expressive capacity, diffusion models have shown great promise in offline RL and imitation learning. Diffusion Actor-Critic with Entropy Regulator (DACER) extended this capability to online RL by using the reverse diffusion process as a policy approximator, trained end-to-end with policy gradient methods, achieving strong performance. However, this comes at the cost of requiring many diffusion steps, which significantly hampers training efficiency, while directly reducing the steps leads to noticeable performance degradation. Critically, the lack of inference efficiency becomes a significant bottleneck for applying diffusion policies in real-time online RL settings. To improve training and inference efficiency while maintaining or even enhancing performance, we propose a Q-gradient field objective as an auxiliary optimization target to guide the denoising process at each diffusion step. Nonetheless, we observe that the independence of the Q-gradient field from the diffusion time step negatively impacts the performance of the diffusion policy. To address this, we introduce a temporal weighting mechanism that enables the model to efficiently eliminate large-scale noise in the early stages and refine actions in the later stages. Experimental results on MuJoCo benchmarks and several multimodal tasks demonstrate that the DACER2 algorithm achieves state-of-the-art performance in most MuJoCo control tasks with only five diffusion steps, while also exhibiting stronger multimodality compared to DACER.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the paper and here's a summary and critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces an enhanced version of the DACER (Diffusion Actor-Critic with Entropy Regulator) algorithm, called DACER2, for online reinforcement learning. DACER uses a diffusion model as a policy approximator, but it's computationally expensive due to the large number of diffusion steps required. DACER2 addresses this inefficiency by proposing a Q-gradient field objective as an auxiliary optimization target to guide the denoising process at each diffusion step.  A time-weighting mechanism is introduced to further improve the process by efficiently removing large-scale noise in the early diffusion stages and refining actions in later stages. The authors demonstrate through experiments on MuJoCo benchmarks that DACER2 achieves state-of-the-art performance with only five diffusion steps, while also exhibiting stronger multimodality compared to DACER. The paper also highlights the faster inference and training efficiency of DACER2 compared to other diffusion-based RL methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Improved Efficiency:** The primary strength lies in significantly improving the efficiency of diffusion-based RL. Reducing the number of diffusion steps from the original DACER is a crucial step towards making these methods more practical for real-time online RL applications. The reported speedups in both training and inference are substantial.
    *   **Maintained or Improved Performance:** The paper claims that DACER2 maintains or even enhances performance compared to DACER, despite using far fewer diffusion steps. This is a significant achievement, as simply reducing steps often leads to performance degradation. The MuJoCo benchmark results appear to support this claim.
    *   **Addresses a Key Bottleneck:** The paper correctly identifies the computational cost of diffusion policies as a major bottleneck and directly tackles this problem. This makes the work relevant and impactful.
    *   **Q-Gradient Field Objective:** The use of the Q-gradient field objective as an auxiliary loss appears to be a key innovation, providing more effective guidance to the diffusion model during training. This novel objective ensures appropriate Q-value guidance along with noise removal guidance.
    *   **Multimodality:** The paper claims and demonstrates that DACER2 displays stronger multimodality than DACER in multimodal benchmark.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the improvements are significant, the core framework still builds upon DACER. The novelty is primarily in the Q-gradient field objective and the time-weighting mechanism, which could be seen as refinements rather than a complete overhaul.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Justification:**  While the paper states and mentions the theory to connect Q-gradient objective with soft policy, the theoretical analysis is relatively high-level. A deeper dive into why the proposed method works so well would strengthen the paper.
    *   **MuJoCo Focus:** The evaluation is heavily focused on MuJoCo benchmarks. While these are standard, demonstrating the method's effectiveness on more diverse and challenging tasks would increase confidence in its generalizability.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** diffusion-based methods usually come with sensitivity with hyperparameter tuning. The table with hyperparameters are not enough to show how hard it is to tune DACER2 for different environments.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper makes a practical contribution to the field of diffusion-based RL by addressing a key computational bottleneck.
    *   The Q-gradient field objective could potentially be applied to other diffusion-based RL algorithms to improve their efficiency.
    *   If the claims of maintained or improved performance with significantly fewer diffusion steps hold up in further research, DACER2 could become a more attractive option for online RL applications.
    *   The improved sample efficiency could potentially reduce the amount of real-world interaction required during training, making the method more applicable to robotic applications.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7**. The paper presents a valuable improvement to an existing algorithm, making diffusion-based RL more practical and efficient. The Q-gradient field objective and the time-weighting mechanism are novel contributions that address a significant bottleneck. However, the work is primarily an incremental improvement upon DACER, and the theoretical justification could be strengthened. While the MuJoCo results are promising, further evaluation on more diverse tasks is needed to fully assess the method's generalizability. The potential of Q-learning based algorithms are not well-justified as the field advances to Transformer-based algorithms. Also, the hyperparameter sensitivity makes the implementation difficult for different applications.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Diversity-Aware Policy Optimization for Large Language Model Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23433v1)
- **Authors**: Jian Yao, Ran Cheng, Xingyu Wu, Jibin Wu, Kay Chen Tan
- **Abstract**: The reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs) have advanced rapidly, particularly following the release of DeepSeek R1, which has inspired a surge of research into data quality and reinforcement learning (RL) algorithms. Despite the pivotal role diversity plays in RL, its influence on LLM reasoning remains largely underexplored. To bridge this gap, this work presents a systematic investigation into the impact of diversity in RL-based training for LLM reasoning, and proposes a novel diversity-aware policy optimization method. Across evaluations on 12 LLMs, we observe a strong positive correlation between the solution diversity and Potential at k (a novel metric quantifying an LLM's reasoning potential) in high-performing models. This finding motivates our method to explicitly promote diversity during RL training. Specifically, we design a token-level diversity and reformulate it into a practical objective, then we selectively apply it to positive samples. Integrated into the R1-zero training framework, our method achieves a 3.5 percent average improvement across four mathematical reasoning benchmarks, while generating more diverse and robust solutions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the role of diversity in reinforcement learning (RL) for training large language models (LLMs) for reasoning tasks. It proposes a diversity-aware policy optimization method, termed R1-zero-Div, that promotes diversity during RL training by introducing a token-level diversity objective applied selectively to positive samples.  The authors first empirically demonstrate a positive correlation between solution diversity and an LLM's "reasoning potential" (quantified by a novel metric, Potential@k) in high-performing models. R1-zero-Div integrates this diversity objective into the R1-zero training framework, resulting in improved performance on mathematical reasoning benchmarks, along with generating more diverse and robust solutions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper provides a formal exploration into the under-explored role of diversity in RL for LLM reasoning. The introduction of Potential@k as a metric to quantify an LLM's reasoning potential (the potential for improvement through RL) is novel and provides a useful way to reason about the impact of RL on LLMs.  The token-level diversity objective is a practical solution to address length bias issues.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The paper's empirical approach is strong.  It systematically evaluates 12 LLMs on mathematical reasoning benchmarks and finds a compelling correlation between diversity and reasoning potential. The ablation studies provide additional insight into the impact of diversity weights and model scale.
*   **Practical Design:** The design of selectively applying the diversity objective to positive samples is well-reasoned. This prevents the diversity enhancement from diluting high quality solutions, thus avoiding the quality-diversity trade-off.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scale of Experiments:** The experimental limitations are a major drawback. The paper acknowledges using a moderate compute setup (8xA6000 GPUs) to scale model. This limits the scope and therefore impact of this work.
*   **Scope of Benchmarks:** The paper focuses solely on mathematical reasoning benchmarks. While this is a well-defined domain, the generalizability of the findings to other reasoning tasks (e.g., commonsense reasoning, logical reasoning) is unclear.
*   **Incremental Contribution:** While the proposed R1-zero-Div method demonstrates improvement over standard R1-zero, its core RL framework remains largely unchanged from previous work. The main contribution is primarily a diversity enhancement objective.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance lies in its emphasis on the importance of diversity in RL for LLM reasoning.  This provides a direction for future work focused on improving reasoning and robustness of LLMs, especially within constrained computational budgets. Furthermore, The Potential@k metric is a useful measure of the possible gains of a model trained with RL that the metric can capture (to some degree).

**Overall:**

The paper addresses an important gap in understanding the benefits of diversity in RL training and it provides a means to measure gains made from it. The methods are well thought-out. However, the contribution is somewhat incremental given the substantial body of existing work in RL fine-tuning of LLMs and it has scope limitations.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The score reflects the good qualities of the paper, along with its lack of scale in terms of resources. The novelty of the diversity analysis is solid and the methodology to implement it is very effective, but is restricted to medium sized models.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### CryoCCD: Conditional Cycle-consistent Diffusion with Biophysical Modeling for Cryo-EM Synthesis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23444v1)
- **Authors**: Runmin Jiang, Genpei Zhang, Yuntian Yang, Siqi Wu, Yuheng Zhang, Wanyue Feng, Yizhou Zhao, Xi Xiao, Xiao Wang, Tianyang Wang, Xingjian Li, Min Xu
- **Abstract**: Cryo-electron microscopy (cryo-EM) offers near-atomic resolution imaging of macromolecules, but developing robust models for downstream analysis is hindered by the scarcity of high-quality annotated data. While synthetic data generation has emerged as a potential solution, existing methods often fail to capture both the structural diversity of biological specimens and the complex, spatially varying noise inherent in cryo-EM imaging. To overcome these limitations, we propose CryoCCD, a synthesis framework that integrates biophysical modeling with generative techniques. Specifically, CryoCCD produces multi-scale cryo-EM micrographs that reflect realistic biophysical variability through compositional heterogeneity, cellular context, and physics-informed imaging. To generate realistic noise, we employ a conditional diffusion model, enhanced by cycle consistency to preserve structural fidelity and mask-aware contrastive learning to capture spatially adaptive noise patterns. Extensive experiments show that CryoCCD generates structurally accurate micrographs and enhances performance in downstream tasks, outperforming state-of-the-art baselines in both particle picking and reconstruction.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "CryoCCD: Conditional Cycle-consistent Diffusion with Biophysical Modeling for Cryo-EM Synthesis" introduces a new method for generating synthetic cryo-EM micrographs.  The method, called CryoCCD, combines biophysical modeling to simulate structurally diverse and realistic cryo-EM images with a conditional cycle-consistent diffusion model to generate realistic noise patterns.  Key components include a modular biophysical engine for generating structurally diverse components within cellular contexts, a conditional diffusion model to produce realistic noise, and cycle consistency to preserve structural fidelity during the synthesis process.  The method uses mask-guided contrastive learning to enhance fine-grained feature representation, outperforming traditional noise models and generative baselines in downstream tasks such as particle picking and pose estimation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**
    * **Biophysical Realism:** The integration of a biophysical engine is a significant strength.  It allows for the creation of synthetic data that captures the complexity of cryo-EM imaging conditions, including compositional heterogeneity and cellular context, which goes beyond simple Gaussian noise models.
    * **Diffusion Model:** The use of a conditional diffusion model is novel and addresses limitations of GAN-based approaches. Diffusion models offer more stable training and better structural fidelity, as noted by the authors, and is further enhanced with a cycle consistency component.
    * **Mask-Guided Contrastive Learning:**  This technique specifically addresses the challenge of high noise in cryo-EM images, enhancing structural discrimination by focusing on relevant particle regions.
    * **Downstream Task Performance:**  The paper provides compelling evidence that CryoCCD enhances performance in particle picking and pose estimation, which are crucial steps in cryo-EM data analysis.  The quantitative results (AUPRC and resolution improvements) support these claims.
    * **Code and Data availability:** The release of CryoCCD as an open-source toolkit will ensure reproducibility of results, and will allow the community to make efficient use of the proposed approach.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on Masks:** The reliance on masks as a conditioning input, as acknowledged by the authors, is a limitation. Obtaining accurate masks can be challenging for real cryo-EM data, which reduces automation capabilities. The paper would have been improved by incorporating other approaches that do not require a mask for conditioning of the diffusion model.
    * **Computational Cost:** While the paper demonstrates performance improvements, it does not address the computational cost associated with the CryoCCD framework, especially given the use of diffusion models. It would have been beneficial to quantify and compare the computational efficiency against existing methods.
    * **Limited Real-World Data Validation:** While the synthetic data leads to improved performance on downstream tasks using real data, there is a lack of direct comparison of generated micrographs with real experimental data, for example through quantitative comparison of noise properties.
    * **Narrow scope of downstream evaluation:** The paper evaluates the performance on only two downstream tasks. Although these are the essential first steps in cryo-EM data processing, a comprehensive study of performance on other tasks like *de novo* structure determination, heterogeneity analysis, and model refinement would have allowed the user to further evaluate the significance of CryoCCD.

* **Novelty and Significance:**
    *  The approach is relatively novel, building upon existing biophysical simulation and generative modeling techniques but integrating them in a way that is specifically tailored for cryo-EM synthesis. The application of diffusion models in this context, along with cycle consistency and mask-guided contrastive learning, contributes significantly to the field. The development provides a method for realistic data augmentation of cryo-EM data.
    * The significance of this work lies in its potential to address the data scarcity problem in cryo-EM. By generating realistic synthetic data, CryoCCD can help improve the training of machine learning models for various downstream tasks. Further, improved model performance in downstream tasks like particle picking and pose estimation can accelerate the cryo-EM structural determination pipeline, enabling insights into biological processes.

* **Justification of Score:**
The paper offers a significant advancement in cryo-EM data synthesis, demonstrating strong performance in downstream tasks. However, the reliance on masks, unknown computational cost, and absence of real-data validation slightly temper its overall impact. The method addresses a real-world problem with a novel and promising technique, which could lead to further research and applications. The paper is very well written and presented and offers significant improvements over current approaches. Considering the strengths and weaknesses discussed above, a score of 7 is justified.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### CMIE: Combining MLLM Insights with External Evidence for Explainable Out-of-Context Misinformation Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23449v1)
- **Authors**: Fanxiao Li, Jiaying Wu, Canyuan He, Wei Zhou
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have demonstrated impressive capabilities in visual reasoning and text generation. While previous studies have explored the application of MLLM for detecting out-of-context (OOC) misinformation, our empirical analysis reveals two persisting challenges of this paradigm. Evaluating the representative GPT-4o model on direct reasoning and evidence augmented reasoning, results indicate that MLLM struggle to capture the deeper relationships-specifically, cases in which the image and text are not directly connected but are associated through underlying semantic links. Moreover, noise in the evidence further impairs detection accuracy. To address these challenges, we propose CMIE, a novel OOC misinformation detection framework that incorporates a Coexistence Relationship Generation (CRG) strategy and an Association Scoring (AS) mechanism. CMIE identifies the underlying coexistence relationships between images and text, and selectively utilizes relevant evidence to enhance misinformation detection. Experimental results demonstrate that our approach outperforms existing methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper, with a score:

**Summary:**

The paper "CMIE: Combining MLLM Insights with External Evidence for Explainable Out-of-Context Misinformation Detection" addresses the problem of detecting out-of-context (OOC) misinformation in multimodal content (images and text). It identifies challenges with existing MLLM-based approaches, specifically the difficulty in capturing deeper semantic relationships and the vulnerability to noisy evidence. To address these, the authors propose CMIE, a framework incorporating a Coexistence Relationship Generation (CRG) strategy to identify underlying relationships between images and text, and an Association Scoring (AS) mechanism to selectively utilize relevant external evidence. Experiments demonstrate that CMIE outperforms existing methods and generates human-readable explanations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the *combination* of techniques for OOC misinformation detection. While MLLMs have been explored, the explicit CRG and AS mechanisms represent a novel approach to improving the accuracy and explainability of misinformation detection. CRG can extract the intrinsic correlation, which makes MLLM focus on the proper validation thread, enhancing the veracity of the following justification.
*   **Significance:** The work addresses a critical real-world problem: the proliferation of multimodal misinformation. The ability to automatically detect and explain OOC misinformation can have a positive impact on social media platforms and public discourse. The emphasis on explainability is particularly important, as it allows users to understand *why* a piece of content is flagged as misinformation, increasing trust and promoting informed decision-making.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing MLLM-based methods for OOC misinformation detection.
    *   **Well-Designed Framework:** The CMIE framework is well-structured and incorporates intuitive and practical solutions to the identified challenges.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:**  The experiments are thorough, comparing CMIE against multiple baselines and conducting ablation studies to assess the contribution of individual components. The cross model evaluation verifies the generality of CMIE.
    *   **Human-Readable Explanations:** The emphasis on generating human-readable explanations is a significant strength. The human evaluation demonstrates the improved quality of explanations.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Hallucinations and Generalization:** The paper acknowledges the potential for MLLM hallucinations, which could affect detection results. Although the prompt engineering and the progressive combination of auxiliary information mitigates this issue, this is not completely avoidable.
    *   **Dataset Dependence:** The experiments are primarily conducted on the NewsCLIPpings dataset. Further evaluation on other diverse datasets and real-world scenarios would strengthen the generalizability of the findings. The limitation may limit the application on the multimodal misinformation domain which is in other languages.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more effective and explainable misinformation detection systems. The CRG and AS mechanisms could be adopted and adapted in other contexts.  The emphasis on explainability aligns with the growing need for transparency and accountability in AI systems.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** I considered the significant challenges of detecting multimodal misinformation and CMIE's capacity to overcome existing obstacles.  The CMIE effectively incorporates and evaluates external data by identifying and scoring the relevant information and then combining the deep and shallow evidence which has achieved significant detection accuracy and enhanced explanation.

**Score: 8**

This score reflects the paper's solid contribution to the field. The combination of CRG and AS mechanisms for enhanced explainability and accuracy makes this work valuable. The limitations of hallucinations and dataset dependence restrict the paper to a score of 8.

- **Classification**: cs.MM
- **Score**: 8/10

### What About Emotions? Guiding Fine-Grained Emotion Extraction from Mobile App Reviews
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23452v1)
- **Authors**: Quim Motger, Marc Oriol, Max Tiessler, Xavier Franch, Jordi Marco
- **Abstract**: Opinion mining plays a vital role in analysing user feedback and extracting insights from textual data. While most research focuses on sentiment polarity (e.g., positive, negative, neutral), fine-grained emotion classification in app reviews remains underexplored. This paper addresses this gap by identifying and addressing the challenges and limitations in fine-grained emotion analysis in the context of app reviews. Our study adapts Plutchik's emotion taxonomy to app reviews by developing a structured annotation framework and dataset. Through an iterative human annotation process, we define clear annotation guidelines and document key challenges in emotion classification. Additionally, we evaluate the feasibility of automating emotion annotation using large language models, assessing their cost-effectiveness and agreement with human-labelled data. Our findings reveal that while large language models significantly reduce manual effort and maintain substantial agreement with human annotators, full automation remains challenging due to the complexity of emotional interpretation. This work contributes to opinion mining by providing structured guidelines, an annotated dataset, and insights for developing automated pipelines to capture the complexity of emotions in app reviews.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the underexplored area of fine-grained emotion extraction from mobile app reviews. It adapts Plutchik's emotion taxonomy to this context, develops annotation guidelines, and creates a dataset of 1,112 annotated sentences. The authors analyze the challenges in human annotation and evaluate the cost-effectiveness and agreement of using Large Language Models (LLMs) for automated emotion annotation. The research provides structured guidelines, an annotated dataset, and insights for developing automated pipelines to capture the complexity of emotions in app reviews. It concludes that while LLMs can reduce manual effort, full automation remains challenging.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its focused application of fine-grained emotion analysis to mobile app reviews. While emotion analysis itself is not new, its detailed application within the specific context of app reviews, including the adaptation of Plutchik's taxonomy and the development of annotation guidelines, makes it a valuable contribution. Furthermore, it offers empirical validation regarding LLM performance in this domain, providing both insights and resources (dataset and guidelines) for other researchers. The novelty, however, is tempered by the fact that similar analyses have been done in other review domains (e.g., product reviews, movie reviews). The application to app reviews is valuable and justified, but doesn't represent a radical departure.
*   **Significance:** The significance is reasonable. The research provides a structured approach and resources to improve user feedback analysis in requirements engineering. By going beyond simple sentiment polarity, it offers more nuanced insights into user needs and experiences. This can lead to better-targeted software development and improved user satisfaction. The analysis of LLMs in this context is practically valuable, as it offers a cost-benefit analysis for researchers and practitioners considering automated solutions.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Structured Methodology:** The iterative human annotation process with clear guidelines and inter-annotator agreement analysis is a strong point.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The evaluation of LLMs covers cost-efficiency and agreement with human annotations, providing a balanced perspective.
    *   **Resources:** The creation and public availability of an annotated dataset and annotation guidelines are valuable contributions to the research community.
    *   **Addresses a Gap:** The study addresses a gap in research, as fine-grained emotion analysis in app reviews is relatively underexplored.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Contribution:** While the application to app reviews is justified, it leverages existing emotion analysis techniques and LLMs, making it an incremental advance rather than a breakthrough.
    *   **Limited Scope:** Although generalizable, the focus specifically on app reviews and the challenges in adapting to alternative review types could be considered a scope limitation. A discussion on how to generalize the proposed approach to other software-related review domains would strengthen the study.
    *   **Threats to Validity:** Although addressed, the heavy reliance on human judgments can introduce subjective biases. Further discussion on the impact of these biases on the results would be beneficial.
    *   **LLM Configuration and Selection Rationale**: While three LLMs are tested, there is no particular rationale in selecting such LLMs to demonstrate the effectiveness of LLM-based automation. Additionally, the LLMs are mostly used with a naive prompt engineering approach. It would be interesting the use of a fine-tunning approach with a subset of our dataset.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in automated sentiment analysis for app reviews and other user feedback domains. The guidelines and dataset can serve as a basis for developing more sophisticated emotion extraction methods. The LLM evaluation provides practical insights for practitioners interested in adopting AI-driven solutions.

**Justification for the Score:**

I assign a score of **6.5**. While the paper makes a valuable contribution by adapting emotion analysis to app reviews, developing detailed guidelines, and providing a public dataset, it doesn't represent a significant theoretical or methodological breakthrough. The use of LLMs, while practically relevant, is an application of existing technology rather than a novel development. The contribution is incremental and application-focused, making it less transformative. However, the practical value of the structured methodology and the released resources should not be understated, therefore deserving above-average scoring.

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 6/10

### Diffusion Guidance Is a Controllable Policy Improvement Operator
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23458v1)
- **Authors**: Kevin Frans, Seohong Park, Pieter Abbeel, Sergey Levine
- **Abstract**: At the core of reinforcement learning is the idea of learning beyond the performance in the data. However, scaling such systems has proven notoriously tricky. In contrast, techniques from generative modeling have proven remarkably scalable and are simple to train. In this work, we combine these strengths, by deriving a direct relation between policy improvement and guidance of diffusion models. The resulting framework, CFGRL, is trained with the simplicity of supervised learning, yet can further improve on the policies in the data. On offline RL tasks, we observe a reliable trend -- increased guidance weighting leads to increased performance. Of particular importance, CFGRL can operate without explicitly learning a value function, allowing us to generalize simple supervised methods (e.g., goal-conditioned behavioral cloning) to further prioritize optimality, gaining performance for "free" across the board.
- **Summary**: Here's a breakdown of the paper and a critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CFGRL (Diffusion Guidance as a Controllable Policy Improvement Operator), a novel framework combining the strengths of generative modeling (diffusion models) and reinforcement learning (RL). CFGRL leverages a direct relationship between policy improvement and the guidance of diffusion models.  Instead of directly optimizing a value function, CFGRL learns an optimality-conditioned policy, allowing it to improve upon policies obtained via supervised methods like goal-conditioned behavioral cloning *without* requiring additional training of a value function. Key benefits include: 1) Training simplicity (supervised learning). 2) Policy improvement beyond the training data. 3) Control over the degree of policy improvement at test time via a guidance weighting parameter. The authors demonstrate CFGRL's effectiveness in both offline RL and goal-conditioned behavioral cloning settings, showing reliable performance improvements and scalability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of linking diffusion model guidance to policy improvement is interesting and potentially novel. The paper's derivation of this connection and its implementation within the CFGRL framework are a significant contribution. While prior works have explored diffusion models in RL, the specific application of classifier-free guidance for *controllable* policy improvement *without* always requiring a value function appears to be a distinct advance. A careful comparison with Kuba et al. [40] is crucial to precisely delineate the novelty of CFGRL. The key distinguishing features would need to lie in the avoidance of explicit A=0, the range of optimality functions supported, no further rejection sampling, and improvement of GCBC without value functions.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact of CFGRL is substantial. The framework addresses a key challenge in RL: how to effectively leverage scalable generative models to improve policies beyond what is present in the data. By enabling simple supervised methods to gain further performance gains this has utility in the field. The practical advantages of CFGRL -- ease of training, scalability, and controllable policy improvement -- make it a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners. The demonstration of CFGRL's effectiveness in both offline RL and goal-conditioned behavioral cloning boosts its significance.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Principled Approach:**  The framework is grounded in a theoretical connection between diffusion guidance and policy improvement, providing a clear justification for the algorithmic design.
    *   **Practical Benefits:**  CFGRL offers several practical advantages, including ease of training, scalability, and the ability to control the degree of policy improvement at test time.
    *   **Broad Applicability:**  The framework is applicable to both offline RL and goal-conditioned behavioral cloning, demonstrating its versatility.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** Experiments across a range of tasks demonstrate the effectiveness of CFGRL, consistently outperforming baseline methods.
    *   **Simple Implementation:** The approach maintains simplicity.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on a Reference Policy:** The approach relies on having a reasonably good reference policy to begin with. Its performance may be limited if the reference policy is very poor.
    *   **Lack of Theoretical Guarantees on Offline Setting:** While the paper provides theoretical justification for the policy improvement framework, it lacks strong theoretical guarantees in the offline RL setting. It would be valuable to provide some theoretical bounds on the sub-optimality of the resulting policy.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** While the paper emphasizes the controllability of CFGRL via the guidance weighting parameter, it's possible that the performance of CFGRL may be sensitive to other hyperparameters, such as the number of diffusion steps.
    *   **Limited Exploration:** The framework has limited exploration capabilities, relying on existing data or a guided diffusion process.
    *   **Comparison Depth**: In Section 5, for CFGRL, the optimality criterion uses only one bit: A(s,a) >= 0 or A(s,a) < 0. Could a continuous (more expressive) version of optimality provide more benefit? And how much benefit does this one-bit of information provide, compared to simply using BC or Flow-BC alone? Similarly, with the flow-matching algorithm, is the benefit from the flow network, or from guidance, or from the combination? More ablation studies on what CFGRL is doing would have been nice.

*   **Potential Influence:** CFGRL has the potential to influence the field in several ways. The framework could inspire new approaches to RL that leverage generative models and classifier-free guidance. It could also lead to the development of new methods for controllable policy improvement. The practical benefits of CFGRL could also make it a valuable tool for practitioners working on real-world RL problems.

**Score:** 7.5/10

**Justification:**

CFGRL presents a significant advancement in combining generative modeling techniques with reinforcement learning for policy improvement. The framework demonstrates strong empirical results and offers practical benefits, making it a valuable contribution to the field.

The novelty lies in its direct connection between diffusion model guidance and policy improvement, and particularly in *controllable* improvement *without always* needing a separately trained value function. The potential influence on the field is substantial, as the method addresses a key challenge in RL and demonstrates improvements in important problems. Therefore, it warrants a high score. The score is lowered slightly due to a few reasons. More thorough ablation studies would have improved confidence in the framework. The offline RL component does not have sufficient theoretical backing. And CFGRL’s dependence on having a reasonably-good prior and exploration limitations are not clearly addressed.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### LAFR: Efficient Diffusion-based Blind Face Restoration via Latent Codebook Alignment Adapter
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23462v1)
- **Authors**: Runyi Li, Bin Chen, Jian Zhang, Radu Timofte
- **Abstract**: Blind face restoration from low-quality (LQ) images is a challenging task that requires not only high-fidelity image reconstruction but also the preservation of facial identity. While diffusion models like Stable Diffusion have shown promise in generating high-quality (HQ) images, their VAE modules are typically trained only on HQ data, resulting in semantic misalignment when encoding LQ inputs. This mismatch significantly weakens the effectiveness of LQ conditions during the denoising process. Existing approaches often tackle this issue by retraining the VAE encoder, which is computationally expensive and memory-intensive. To address this limitation efficiently, we propose LAFR (Latent Alignment for Face Restoration), a novel codebook-based latent space adapter that aligns the latent distribution of LQ images with that of HQ counterparts, enabling semantically consistent diffusion sampling without altering the original VAE. To further enhance identity preservation, we introduce a multi-level restoration loss that combines constraints from identity embeddings and facial structural priors. Additionally, by leveraging the inherent structural regularity of facial images, we show that lightweight finetuning of diffusion prior on just 0.9% of FFHQ dataset is sufficient to achieve results comparable to state-of-the-art methods, reduce training time by 70%. Extensive experiments on both synthetic and real-world face restoration benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness and efficiency of LAFR, achieving high-quality, identity-preserving face reconstruction from severely degraded inputs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "LAFR: Efficient Diffusion-based Blind Face Restoration via Latent Codebook Alignment Adapter" addresses the problem of blind face restoration from low-quality images. It proposes a novel approach using a latent codebook alignment adapter (LAFR) to align the latent distribution of low-quality (LQ) images with that of high-quality (HQ) images within a diffusion model framework. The key idea is to correct the semantic misalignment that occurs when encoding LQ images using a VAE trained on HQ data. The method also introduces a multi-level restoration loss to further enhance identity preservation. The authors claim that their approach achieves state-of-the-art performance with minimal computational cost and parameter count by efficiently adapting a pre-trained diffusion prior using only a small subset of a face dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a moderately novel approach. While diffusion models and latent space alignment have been explored in image restoration, the specific combination of a lightweight codebook-based adapter with a multi-level loss function for efficient fine-tuning in blind face restoration demonstrates novelty. The idea of using a small subset of FFHQ data and pruning the UNet for efficient adaptation is interesting.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies primarily in its efficiency and resource-effectiveness. Existing methods for addressing latent space misalignment often involve computationally expensive retraining or complex alignment modules. The proposed method provides a more lightweight and efficient alternative, which could make it more accessible for applications with limited computational resources. Also, demonstrates that pre-trained large models can be effectively used in specific tasks by fine-tuning on a tiny subset of the data.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Efficiency:** The paper emphasizes and demonstrates efficiency through reduced training time and parameter count, making it a practical solution.
    *   **Performance:** The experimental results on both synthetic and real-world datasets demonstrate competitive or superior performance compared to existing methods.
    *   **Clear problem definition:** The paper clearly identifies and addresses the problem of latent space misalignment.
    *   **Ablation studies:** Thorough ablation studies are performed to validate the contribution of each component of the proposed method.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental advancement:** While the combination is novel, each component builds upon existing techniques. The codebook alignment adapter is inspired by existing adapter architectures, and the loss functions are based on established perceptual and identity losses.
    *   **Dataset Dependency:** Although authors argue that the method could be generalized with a small number of face images, experiments have been conducted on FFHQ images, demonstrating high dependency.

* **Impact:**
    * This paper offers an efficient way to apply the general diffusion models to a specific task. This may inspire future studies to explore the possibility of task-specific training of large pre-trained models to improve the efficiency and practicality.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper offers a worthwhile contribution to the field of blind face restoration. While the individual components are not entirely groundbreaking, the specific combination and the focus on efficiency make this a valuable study. However, the degree of novelty is not at the level of breakthrough, therefore a score of 7 out of 10 is justified.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Synthesizing Performance Constraints for Evaluating and Improving Code Efficiency
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23471v1)
- **Authors**: Jun Yang, Cheng-Chi Wang, Bogdan Alexandru Stoica, Kexin Pei
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have been increasingly used to optimize code efficiency. Evaluating their effectiveness and further suggesting optimization opportunities often rely on high-quality tests to demonstrate the performance bottlenecks presented in the program. However, existing approaches rely on a limited set of hand-curated inputs or LLM-generated uninteresting length-stressing tests, failing to reveal more nuanced optimization opportunities. We present WEDGE, a framework for generating performance-stressing input given the program under test. WEDGE synthesizes explicit performance-characterizing constraints in the form of branch conditions to partition the programs' execution space into performance-specific regions. When integrated with the coverage-guided fuzzer, reaching different regions introduces explicit rewards for test generation to explore inefficient implementations. Our evaluation shows that WEDGE introduces a significant slowdown compared to the tests in CodeContests and those claimed to be optimized by existing approaches. From the utility perspective, integrating our tests substantially improves the existing code optimization approaches that rely on test-driven execution feedback. We release PERFFORGE, the performance tests generated by WEDGE, to benchmark future approaches for efficient code generation at https://github.com/UChiSeclab/perfforge.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Synthesizing Performance Constraints for Evaluating and Improving Code Efficiency":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces WEDGE, a novel framework for generating performance-stressing test inputs for code efficiency evaluation, particularly focusing on code optimized by Large Language Models (LLMs). WEDGE operates by synthesizing explicit performance-characterizing constraints in the form of branch conditions. These constraints partition a program's execution space into performance-specific regions. When integrated with a coverage-guided fuzzer, reaching different regions introduces explicit rewards for test generation to explore inefficient implementations.  The evaluation demonstrates that WEDGE-generated tests induce significant slowdown compared to existing tests, including CodeContests tests and those generated by LLM-based approaches like EvalPerf and TG-prompt. Furthermore, these tests improve code optimization approaches that rely on test-driven feedback. The authors release PERFFORGE, a benchmark of WEDGE-generated performance tests.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's core novelty lies in its *approach* to performance test generation. Existing methods often either use limited hand-crafted tests or rely on LLMs to generate generic, length-stressing inputs.  WEDGE, however, strategically uses LLMs to identify *performance-characterizing constraints* and then employs fuzzing to generate inputs that specifically satisfy these constraints. This decoupling of constraint reasoning from direct input generation seems to be the key to its success.  This is a significant departure from existing, direct LLM-based test input generation approaches.

*   **Significance:** The significance is multi-faceted:

    *   **Better Performance Testing:** The paper convincingly argues that WEDGE generates *more effective* performance stress tests. The significant slowdowns achieved compared to existing baselines strongly support this claim. This has important implications for reliably evaluating and improving code optimization techniques.
    *   **Improved LLM-Based Optimization:**  The evaluation shows that WEDGE-generated tests, when used in conjunction with iterative code optimization methods (like EFFI-LEARNER), lead to *greater improvements in code efficiency*. This demonstrates the practical utility of the generated tests.
    *   **A Useful Benchmark:** The release of PERFFORGE is a valuable contribution. It provides a challenging benchmark for future research on code efficiency evaluation and automated test generation.
    *   **A Promising Direction:** The framework itself, decoupling LLM-based constraint discovery from search, points to a promising future direction for combining reasoning and search techniques.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the problem of performance test generation and articulates the limitations of existing approaches.
    *   **Well-Defined Methodology:** WEDGE is presented as a well-structured framework with clearly defined components (profiling, constraint synthesis, guided fuzzing).
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The evaluation includes comparisons to relevant baselines, ablation studies, and utility evaluations. The use of CPU instruction counts as a reliable metric is justified.
    *   **Release of PERFFORGE:** A great move towards reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **LLM Cost and Overhead:**  The framework relies on LLMs for constraint synthesis and mutator generation, which introduces computational overhead and potentially significant monetary cost (token usage). While the evaluation shows the effectiveness, a discussion about the cost-benefit trade-offs is missing. The paper touches on the issue, but it needs more emphasis.
    *   **Input Length Limitation:** While the paper addresses the weakness of length stressing and highlights how WEDGE is not solely based on large inputs it also mentions limitations with AFL++ and its mutation of large inputs.  This input size limitation could impact the generalizability to problems requiring very large inputs.
    *   **Heuristics:** The paper presents a solid framework but contains several heuristics, such as contrastive input pair mining or the threshold of totalBoxes for the C++ checker functions as seen in listing 13.
    *   **Limited Scope (for now):** The evaluation is largely limited to C++ and Python solutions from CodeContests. While this dataset provides a good starting point, future work should explore the applicability of WEDGE to other languages and more complex, real-world codebases.

*   **Potential Influence:** WEDGE has the potential to significantly influence the field of code efficiency evaluation and automated test generation. It provides a practical and effective approach for generating challenging performance tests, which can be used to improve code optimization techniques and identify performance bottlenecks.  The release of PERFFORGE will also facilitate further research in this area. It is also more useful than prior benchmarks with limited scope and low complexity.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a significant contribution to the field with a novel and well-executed approach to performance test generation. While there are some limitations related to computational cost and reliance on heuristics, the overall impact of WEDGE is substantial. The framework is well-defined, the evaluation is thorough, and the release of PERFFORGE is a valuable resource for the community. For all these reasons, I am assigning a score of 8 out of 10.
Score: 8
- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### EVOREFUSE: Evolutionary Prompt Optimization for Evaluation and Mitigation of LLM Over-Refusal to Pseudo-Malicious Instructions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23473v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaorui Wu, Xiaofeng Mao, Fei Li, Xin Zhang, Xiaolu Zhang, Jun Zhou, Yuxiang Peng, Li Zheng, Chong Teng, Donghong Ji, Zhuang Li
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) frequently refuse to respond to pseudo-malicious instructions: semantically harmless input queries triggering unnecessary LLM refusals due to conservative safety alignment, significantly impairing user experience. Collecting such instructions is crucial for evaluating and mitigating over-refusals, but existing instruction curation methods, like manual creation or instruction rewriting, either lack scalability or fail to produce sufficiently diverse and effective refusal-inducing prompts. To address these limitations, we introduce EVOREFUSE, a prompt optimization approach that generates diverse pseudo-malicious instructions consistently eliciting confident refusals across LLMs. EVOREFUSE employs an evolutionary algorithm exploring the instruction space in more diverse directions than existing methods via mutation strategies and recombination, and iteratively evolves seed instructions to maximize evidence lower bound on LLM refusal probability. Using EVOREFUSE, we create two novel datasets: EVOREFUSE-TEST, a benchmark of 582 pseudo-malicious instructions that outperforms the next-best benchmark with 140.41% higher average refusal triggering rate across 9 LLMs, 34.86% greater lexical diversity, and 40.03% improved LLM response confidence scores; and EVOREFUSE-ALIGN, which provides 3,000 pseudo-malicious instructions with responses for supervised and preference-based alignment training. LLAMA3.1-8B-INSTRUCT supervisedly fine-tuned on EVOREFUSE-ALIGN achieves up to 14.31% fewer over-refusals than models trained on the second-best alignment dataset, without compromising safety. Our analysis with EVOREFUSE-TEST reveals models trigger over-refusals by overly focusing on sensitive keywords while ignoring broader context.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "EVOREFUSE: Evolutionary Prompt Optimization for Evaluation and Mitigation of LLM Over-Refusal to Pseudo-Malicious Instructions."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EVOREFUSE, an evolutionary prompt optimization algorithm designed to generate diverse "pseudo-malicious" instructions. These are semantically harmless queries that, due to overly conservative safety alignment, trigger unnecessary refusals from Large Language Models (LLMs). EVOREFUSE uses an evolutionary algorithm, maximizing an Evidence Lower Bound (ELBO) on LLM refusal probability to achieve both high refusal rates and instruction diversity. The algorithm uses mutation and recombination operations, guided by ELBO-based fitness evaluation and safety verification. The authors create two new datasets using EVOREFUSE: EVOREFUSE-TEST (a benchmark for over-refusal evaluation) and EVOREFUSE-ALIGN (a dataset for alignment training to mitigate over-refusal). Experiments show that EVOREFUSE-TEST outperforms existing benchmarks and models fine-tuned with EVOREFUSE-ALIGN exhibit reduced over-refusal rates. The paper also analyzes the causes of over-refusal, attributing it to shortcut learning on sensitive keywords.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the EVOREFUSE algorithm and its application to the over-refusal problem. While evolutionary algorithms and prompt optimization techniques have been explored before in the context of LLMs, the specific combination of an ELBO-based fitness function, guided mutation/recombination operations informed by existing over-refusal patterns, and a focus on generating diverse pseudo-malicious instructions is relatively novel. The creation of the two datasets, EVOREFUSE-TEST and EVOREFUSE-ALIGN, also represents a valuable contribution. The analysis of shortcut learning and early-layer biases provides a deeper understanding of the over-refusal phenomenon.

*   **Significance:** The over-refusal problem is a practical concern that degrades the user experience with LLMs. By providing a method for generating challenging test cases (EVOREFUSE-TEST) and a dataset for mitigating over-refusal (EVOREFUSE-ALIGN), the paper addresses a real-world issue. Demonstrating the effectiveness of EVOREFUSE in reducing over-refusals without sacrificing safety is a significant result. The analysis of the causes of over-refusal (shortcut learning) offers insights that can inform future safety alignment strategies.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the problem of over-refusal and motivates its importance.
